# Ledger — vanilla OpenSees files we touched

Every upstream ("vanilla") OpenSees file the Ladruno fork modifies, **why** we
touched it, and the **PR** it landed in. This is the provenance record: if we
ever rebase onto a newer upstream, this table is the list of edits to re-apply
and re-verify.

## Conventions

- **Vanilla = pre-existing upstream file.** Brand-new files we author live in
  [[LEDGER_implementations]] instead — do not list them here.
- Keep edits minimal and marked. Every Ladruno edit in a vanilla file carries a
  `// Ladruno ...` comment so `grep -rn "Ladruno" SRC/` reconstructs this table.
- One row per (file, PR). If the same file is touched by several PRs, add a row
  per PR so the history stays linear.
- PR numbers are on the fork: `github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees` (branch `ladruno`).
- When you touch a new vanilla file, **add the row in the same PR**.

## Ledger

| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesOutputCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno: ADR-87 D2`: adds `OPS_LadrunoMutation()` — reports which physics family this binary was deliberately sabotaged for ("none" for a normal build). The mutation gate's wrong-build detector: without it a silently-failed mutant build would re-run the previous binary, every test would pass, and the gate would report the OPPOSITE of the truth. | WP-2 |

| `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp` | `// Ladruno` ADR-43 (apeGmsh ADR 0077): classic-Tcl parity for band-targeted FEAST — `eigenAnalysis()` gains a `-feast fmin fmax [-m0/-nq/-tol/-maxiter/-verbose/-certify/-blockZGate/-rci]` branch mirroring the openseespy `OPS_eigenFeast` (parsed up front since the band replaces `numModes`, so it can't share the ARPACK arg shape), building `FeastEigenSOE`/`FeastEigenSolver` + the found-mode reconcile (`getNumFoundModes()` trim of the domain eigenvalue list). Makes `eigen -feast` reachable from classic `OpenSees.exe` (serial PARDISO) and `OpenSeesMP.exe` (distributed dmumps L3 via `LadrunoDistBlockZKernel`, `_PARALLEL_INTERPRETERS`) — previously openseespy/PyMP-only (the deck target apeGmsh's partitioned-Tcl HPC path needs). Adversarial-review hardening: (1) repeated `eigen -feast` in one interpreter **mutates the existing SOE in place** (re-applies band + every param w/ ctor defaults) — the classic reuse-on-classTag-match pattern would silently solve call 2 with call 1's band, and forcing a fresh SOE is a trap (the persistent analysis's `setEigenSOE` also keeps its OLD SOE on classTag match, dropping the new one); (2) a FEAST failure (incl. a `-certify` REFUSE) returns `TCL_ERROR` instead of upstream's swallowed fall-through (ARPACK failure behavior kept byte-identical). +`#include <FeastEigenSOE.h>`/`<FeastEigenSolver.h>`. Additive; non-`-feast` `eigen` byte-identical. | — |

Optional value via the safe peek-`OPS_GetString`-then-`OPS_ResetCurrentInputArg(-1)` idiom (string read consumes on both Tcl + Py — dodges the failed-numeric-rewind quirk). REFUSED with `-mortar` (NTS-only) and without a base penalty (a modifier — needs a positional `auto`/`kn`; that base kn is what an implicit run uses). `>1` warns (`ω·dt=2√SOFSCL>2` unstable). Off (default) ⇒ byte-identical; explicit-only ⇒ implicit byte-identical. Additive; existing invocations unchanged. | [#402](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/402) |
| `SRC/domain/domain/Domain.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno` ADR-41 D1: add a `bool contactAugmenting` flag (in-class `=false` + init `false` in all 4 ctors; declared last → no `-Wreorder`) + inline `set/isContactAugmenting`. In `Domain::commit()` the recorder loop + `commitTag++` are wrapped in `if (!contactAugmenting) { … }` so a held-load within-step augmentation sweep (`analyze_augmented` at a zero load increment) fires NO recorders and bumps NO commitTag — the contact Uzawa λ update (`theContactDomain->commit()`) and `committedTime=currentTime` still run (committedTime a no-op under the zero-increment LoadControl). Flag OFF (default) ⇒ `commit()` byte-identical to stock. Driven by `ladrunoBeginAugment/EndAugment`. | [#411](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/411) |
| `SRC/interpreter/{OpenSeesOutputCommands.cpp,OpenSeesCommands.h,PythonWrapper.cpp,TclWrapper.cpp}` | `// Ladruno` ADR-41 D1: add the `ladrunoBeginAugment` / `ladrunoEndAugment` commands → `OPS_LadrunoBeginAugment`/`EndAugment` set `Domain::setContactAugmenting(true/false)` (open/close a held-load within-step augmentation sweep; no args, null-domain guarded, idempotent). OPS_ bodies + decls + Py (`Py_ops_LadrunoBeginAugment`/`EndAugment`) + Tcl (`Tcl_ops_…`) wrappers + dual `addCommand`. Additive; no existing command touched. | [#411](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/411) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesOutputCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno` ADR-41 D2.2/SOFT=2: relax the `-mortar -soft` refusal to ALLOW `-visc <μ_c>` (the D2.2 viscous normal damper on the SOFT=2 active set) while still refusing `-tie/-mu/-cohesion/-tauMax`. | [#412](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/412) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesOutputCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno` SOFT=2 friction: relax the `-mortar -soft` refusal further to ALLOW `-mu/-cohesion/-tauMax` (Courant-stable Coulomb/Tresca on the SOFT=2 overlap); now ONLY `-tie` is refused with `-soft`. | [#414](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/414) |
| `SRC/analysis/fe_ele/FE_Element.cpp` | `// Ladruno` ADR-39 P2a: in the subtype ctor `FE_Element(tag, numDOF_Group, ndof)`, move `numFEs++` BELOW the `if (numFEs == 0)` class-wide-scratch allocation guard (was incremented first ⇒ guard dead). A model whose ONLY FE_Elements are subtype adapters (a contact-only model with no Domain Elements) never allocated `theMatrices`/`theVectors` ⇒ null-deref in `~FE_Element` when the last FE was destroyed (teardown segfault). Zero behaviour change for any model with an element-backed FE (numFEs already >0 there); fixes all FE subtypes (PenaltySP_FE etc.), not just contact. | `SRC/analysis/analysis/DirectIntegrationAnalysis.cpp` | `// Ladruno` ADR-30: `domainChanged()` now honors the `ConstraintHandler::handle()` / `doneNumberingDOF()` / `Integrator::domainChanged()` error contract (`<0` ⇒ return −1); same guard added to the `setAlgorithm()` / `setIntegrator()` setter paths (mid-session swap). Upstream ignored these returns, so a handler/integrator that DETECTS a bad setup (the projection handler's chain / double-constraint / SP-on-slave / massless / IC-compliance diagnostics, non-projection-aware-integrator refusal) printed its named error but the analysis ran on regardless. Additive — `≥0` on success, `<0` only on a genuine error (verified vs full Zone-A, 0 regressions). | [#305](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/305) |

| `SRC/domain/pattern/drm/H5DRMLoadPattern.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (DRM study, integrating jaabell `merged-Explicit-ASDP-master`): (1) **Z-flip removed** — commented out `d[2]=-d[2]`/`a[2]=-a[2]` in both read paths (jaabell 9be15c3d0); validated CORRECT for a z-down model frame (interior uz tracks prescribed Dn, corr +0.98, ratio ~1.0). (2) **Hold-final-displacement past tend** (jaabell 99b7c2c11) — guard `t<tstart||t>tend` → `t<tstart` + a `t>=tend` branch that reads the final displacement timestep and holds it with zero accel; + the i2 near-end clamp (`i2>=N_timesteps-2`). Fixes the tend index-overrun that hit `errorflag3/4<0` → "Failed to read" → `exit(-1)`. **Reuses the persistent member `ih5_dis_ds` (acquired in do_intitialization, closed in cleanup) so the amnp95 memory-leak fix is preserved** (no per-call H5Dget_space/double-close). Mirrors the legacy `SRC/domain/pattern/TclPatternCommand.cpp` parser (which itself leaves `stuff[12]` uninitialized when <12 transform args given — same latent bug, fixed here by the identity default). | `SRC/domain/pattern/TclPatternCommand.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: initialize the `pattern H5DRM` transform array `stuff[12]` to identity-T + zero-x0. This is the ACTIVE Tcl `pattern` parser for the `OpenSees.exe` interpreter (the latent bug predicted in the pattern.cpp row above). | `SRC/analysis/integrator/TransientIntegrator.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-77 T0`: **supersedes the "DOF_Group loop is left untimed" decision in the #49 row above.** Adds a coarse `OPS_PROFILE_SCOPE("dof.tangent")` around the DOF_Group `addA` loop in `formTangent`, re-braced to scope it. This loop carries the **nodal** mass/damping into A and exists ONLY on the transient path (`IncrementalIntegrator::formTangent` has no DOF_Group loop at all) — leaving it untimed silently folded nodal-mass assembly into "the rest of `formTangent`", which is precisely the five-loop gap ADR-75 P1f flagged. CentralDifferenceLadruno scatters `M(a_raw−a_proj)` here each commit so the node-based LadrunoRecorder can record a `constraintTieForce` field. | `SRC/element/Element.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno` (ADR 20 §10.6.1): add `virtual double getExplicitCriticalTimeStep()` to the `Element` base — default returns −1 ("no opinion"). Lets an element self-report an explicit critical step its per-element `K v = λ M v` pencil can't express; overridden by `LadrunoEmbeddedRebar` to surface its bipenalty bound `2√(m_p/k_eff)` (invisible to the eigensolve, which sees the massless-host coupling as `λ_max=0`). Additive base-class virtual (vtable change ⇒ recompile-all, no existing behavior touched). | [#186](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/186) |
| `SRC/element/Element.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno` (ADR 23 §3, Phase 2 UR): add `virtual int getInterpolationGradients(const Vector& xi, Matrix& dNdx)` to the `Element` base — default returns −1 (not implemented). The gradient companion of `getInterpolationWeights`: fills `dNdx(i,j)=∂N_i/∂x_j` so `LadrunoEmbeddedNode`'s rotation (UR) tie can read the host continuum rotation `θ=½curl(u)=skew(∇u)` at the embedded point (weights alone can't — the rotation needs `∂N/∂x`). Overridden by fork hosts `LadrunoBrick` (via `shp3d`) + `BezierTet10` (via `computeJacobian`). Additive base-class virtual (vtable change ⇒ recompile-all, no existing behavior touched). | [#208](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/208) |
| `SRC/analysis/integrator/CriticalTimeStep.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (ADR 20 §10.6.1): at the top of the per-element loop in `computeCriticalTimeStep`, query `ele->getExplicitCriticalTimeStep()`; a non-negative value is folded into the running damped/undamped minima and the per-element eigensolve is skipped for that element. Default −1 ⇒ every existing element takes the unchanged eigensolve path. Makes `ops.criticalTimeStep`/`-cflAbort` honor a self-reporting element's bound. | [#186](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/186) |

| `SRC/material/nD/InitStrainNDMaterial.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno`: make Petracca's fixed-prestrain `InitStrain` wrapper **dimension-general**. Was 3D-only — `getOrder()` hardcoded 6 and `getCopy(type)` only answered `"ThreeDimensional"`, so the base `NDMaterial::getCopy()` returned `0` for `PlaneStrain`/`AxiSymmetric` (the **default** view for `LadrunoQuad`/`LadrunoCST`) → null material → element construction failed. Fix: add an adopting ctor that preserves an already type-reduced inner view; `getCopy(type)` builds `PlaneStrain`/`AxiSymmetric` natively (inner = that view of the stored 3D material; `eps0` reduced via the LadrunoJ2 `vmap` {0,1,3} / {0,1,2,3}); `getOrder()` delegates to the inner; `setTrialStrain(Incr)` sized to the inner order; `getCopy()` clones preserving the current view. Keeps a canonical size-6 `epsInit3D` so the `eps0_ij` setParameter API has stable indices in every view (updateParameter edits 3D + re-projects; out-of-view comps are no-ops, no OOB). `sendSelf/recvSelf` carry `epsInit3D`+active order. `PlaneStress`/`PlateFiber`/`BeamFiber`/3D unchanged (legacy base-wrapper path). Additive — 3D behavior byte-identical. | [#222](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/222) |

| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/umfGEN/UmfpackGenLinSolver.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno` ADR-40 rank 8/10: **Numeric-persist factorization reuse.** Persist the UMFPACK `Numeric` LU as a solver member and reuse it (trisolve only) while `theSOE->factored` says the assembled matrix is unchanged; refactor iff the tangent was reassembled / structure changed / no valid Numeric held. `freeNumeric()` on setSize (stale vs new Symbolic), on any factor/solve error, and in the dtor. `-noNumericPersist` restores the legacy free-every-solve behavior (bit-identical). | `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/umfGEN/UmfpackGenLinSOE.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1g`: **`addA` scatter — linear scan → binary search**, the UmfPack half of the fix ADR-75 P1f ([#636](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/636)) made for PARDISO. The stock inner loop rescanned the whole CSC column for every one of `idSize²` element entries, i.e. `O(idSize² × collen)`. ADR-75b's L3-0 profile measured it at **1322.8 ms of a ~19.2 s Lane-B step** and named it as the remaining half of the finding. New `ops_umfpack_findRow()` binary search over `Ai[Ap[col] .. Ap[col+1])`. **Legal because each column is strictly ascending — and that is now ENFORCED, not assumed:** it holds by construction (every column is built through `ID::insert`, a binary-search insertion into a sorted array with dedup), and `setSize` now also CHECKS it and returns −1 with a loud message otherwise. The check is O(nnz) once per `setSize` against an assembly loop that runs for the whole analysis — free, and it converts a future silent breakage into a loud one. Verified by brute force before building: **2.9 M probes over random ascending columns — empty columns, absent rows, out-of-range — 0 mismatches vs the linear scan.** Also mirrors P1f's **free miss detector**: the `k >= 0` test already exists, so `else missing = 1` costs a never-taken branch and turns "an element entry has no CSC slot ⇒ its stiffness is silently discarded" from undetectable into a once-per-SOE warning. **MEASURED** (same interleaved harness as P1f, `MKL_NUM_THREADS=1` so the exactness check is meaningful — see the threaded-PARDISO determinism quirk; two independent runs): **1.044–1.057× at 11.5k DOF, 1.031–1.027× at 26.5k**, `ux` **bit-identical in all four size × run combinations**. ⚠ **The win is SMALLER than PARDISO's and SHRINKS with size** (P1f got 1.098× at 26.5k, growing). That is not a defect, it is the same insight in mirror image: `addA` pays in inverse proportion to how expensive the solve already is. UmfPack scales ~O(N²) (P1c), so its `addA` is a *shrinking* fraction of runtime — whereas P1f's own rationale was "this was the right next lever precisely BECAUSE the solver work landed". **So this is a consistency/robustness win on the fallback solver, not a headline; the desktop recommendation remains `system Pardiso`.** <br><br>Prior edit — `// Ladruno` ADR-40 rank 8/10: add the standard `bool factored` invalidation flag (the 25-year `BandGenLinSOE`/`ProfileSPD`/`FullGen` idiom UMFPACK alone never wired into) — init false, reset false in `zeroA()` (always precedes tangent reassembly) and `setSize(Graph&)`; the solver sets it true after a successful factorization. Load-bearing invariant: `factored==true ⟺ persisted Numeric = LU(current Ax)`. | ADR-40 rank 8/10 |

Strictly additive — force path untouched. | ADR-69 P1 |

First valid commit seeds only (the step's start time predates the first modal `formUnbalance`). `mdRateValid` cleared per commit so a stale rate can never re-publish; failed/reverted steps never publish. 5 in-class-initialized scratch members in the header. | `SRC/element/absorbentBoundaries/ASDAbsorbingBoundary2D.{h,cpp}`, `ASDAbsorbingBoundary3D.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno` ADR-69 P1.5: BOTTOM compliant-base incident-injection leak publisher — new `commitState()` override (neither class had one; falls through to `Element::commitState()`), same pattern as `LysmerTriangle`: recompute the injection force via the element's own `addBaseActions(Vector&)` (a pure external source — time-series velocity × fixed coefficients, always additive into `R`, no stateful-member hazard unlike Lysmer) dotted with `getVelocity()` (same index space `addBaseActions` writes into via `m_dof_map`), trapezoid-integrate per commit, publish to `ABSORB_LEAK`. Gated on `m_stage != Stage_StaticConstraint && (m_boundary & BND_BOTTOM)` — verified `addRff`/`addRffToSoil` (the LATERAL free-field mechanism) both `return` early on `BND_BOTTOM`, so the two source terms are mutually exclusive and publishing one cannot double-count the other. 3 diagnostic members (`m_lkPrevRate/m_lkLastTime/m_lkSeeded`) per class, in-class-initialized to match the header's existing style, deliberately not serialized. **P1.6**: the publisher branch now covers LATERAL instances too — `!(m_boundary & BND_BOTTOM)` recomputes `addRffToSoil(Vector&)` (a pure function of `getTrialDisp()`, unchanged between last residual formation and commit; writes only soil-side dofs, zeros on FF-column dofs, so the full `^ getVelocity()` dot picks up exactly the soil-transfer power) into the same trapezoid → `ABSORB_LEAK` machinery; per-instance state can't collide because an instance is bottom-XOR-lateral. | ADR-69 P1.5, P1.6 |

Adds an `MPI_Comm theComm` member (defaults WORLD → default behavior byte-identical) + `setCommunicator(MPI_Comm)` (tears down any live MUMPS instance, forces re-init); `initializeMumps()` now uses `theComm` for `comm_fortran` and rank/size. | `SRC/element/Element.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: **the `index == -1` scratch-slot self-heal called the VIRTUAL `setRayleighDampingFactors`** at all **11** sites in this file (`getResistingForceIncInertia`, `getRayleighDampingForces`, the 5 `*Sensitivity` getters, `getGeometricTangentStiff`, …). Every one of them dereferences `theMatrices[index]` / `theVectors1[index]` on the very next line, so an element that **overrides** `setRayleighDampingFactors` to ignore Rayleigh damping never reaches the base allocator, `index` stays `-1`, and the result is an **access violation, not a wrong number**. Six fork elements override it exactly that way (`LadrunoUP`, `LadrunoRigidBody`, and the four penalty ties `LadrunoEmbeddedNode` / `LadrunoEmbeddedRebar` / `LadrunoKinematicCoupling` / `LadrunoDistributingCoupling`). Fix is one qualifier per site — `this->Element::setRayleighDampingFactors(...)` — so the base always allocates its own slot; the stored factors are untouched, so an element that ignores Rayleigh reports a **zero** damping force, which is what it means. **Found by the recorder-token sweep** (see [[LEDGER_implementations]]): the sweep made all fork elements chain to `Element::setResponse`, which routes `dampingForce` → `Element::getResponse(222222)` → `getRayleighDampingForces()`, and `tests/test_ladruno_response_tokens.py::test_up_tokens` crashed the interpreter on the first run. Latent before that only because nothing reached the path — but it was **never** fork-specific: **upstream's own `Subdomain` (an `Element` subclass) overrides it as a pure forwarder to `Domain::setRayleighDampingFactors`**, which likewise never sets `Element::index`, so vanilla OpenSees carries the identical crash. Only behavioural delta: the self-heal no longer re-propagates the factors into a `Subdomain`'s interior — a no-op, since it passes back the values already stored. | [#676](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/676) |

| `SRC/element/fourNodeQuad/{FourNodeQuad,FourNodeQuad3d,NineNodeQuad,EightNodeQuad,SixNodeTri}.cpp` + `SRC/element/triangle/Tri31.cpp` | `// Ladruno` **upstream bug fix**: these continuum elements gate the mass matrix on `if (rho == 0) use material density; else use element rho`, but `sendSelf`/`recvSelf` never serialized the element-level `rho`, and the no-arg (broker) ctor left it UNINITIALIZED (only `FourNodeQuad3d`'s ctor already zeroed it). On `database`/`restore` (and OpenSeesMP send) the element `rho` was garbage heap → garbage≠0 hijacked the mass → NON-deterministic, indefinite M (negative generalized eigenvalues), diverging transient restarts. Fix (A+B, strictly additive): append `rho` to the send/recv data Vector (+1 slot) and add `rho(0.0)` to each blank ctor. Committed nodal disp round-trips fine, so it was invisible to a plain nodeDisp `database_roundtrip`; caught by `tests/test_quad_tri_rho_db_restart.py` (continues the transient across the restart). Vanilla bug — affects all mainline users doing DB/parallel dynamic restarts; upstream candidate. | [#577](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/577) |
| `SRC/domain/domain/Domain.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (ADR-69/ADR-72 P3): `clearAll()` now calls `Ladruno::EnergyChannelRegistry::instance().resetOnWipe()` (+include). The ADR-69 energy-channel registry kept PROCESS-lifetime totals; a diverged explicit run with LNVD active published non-finite work and poisoned RES for every later model in the process (NaN−NaN survives the recorder's baseline subtraction — Linux Zone-A, PR #584 r1), and a huge-but-finite total (~1e300 pre-overflow) ABSORBS a later model's small increments (`total + dE == total` in double precision), silently zeroing its channel delta. wipe() destroys every producer/consumer, so `clearAll` is the semantic zero point. Strictly additive; pairs with the `addEnergy` finiteness guard in fork-owned `LadrunoEnergyChannels.h`. Regression: `tests/test_ladrunoBrick20_dynamics.py::test_energy_registry_survives_prior_diverged_lnvd_run`. | [#584](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/584) |

| `SRC/element/tetrahedron/TenNodeTetrahedron.cpp` | `// Ladruno BUGFIX` ×2 — **two independent upstream defects in jaabell's own element**, both now marked. **(a) `shp3d` 6× stiffness bug** (found by the 2026-07-22 upstream-campaign audit; landed pre-campaign in `ee942546c` but left UNMARKED/UNLEDGERED until this PR): `Jdet` already carries the tetrahedral 1/6 factor (it is the element *volume*), and the Gauss weights `wg[]` also sum to 1/6, so `xsj = Jdet` applied 1/6 TWICE → every stiffness/reaction 6× too soft (uniaxial patch test: reaction = E·ε·A/6). Fix `xsj = 6.0*Jdet` (derivative shape fns use 1/(6·Jdet) and are unaffected). **(b) `getResponse` 144-byte heap overrun** — `static Vector stresses(6)` was copy-pasted from `FourNodeTetrahedron` (1 GP ⇒ 6 is correct there), but BOTH the `stresses` (responseID 3) and `strains` (responseID 4) branches loop over all `NumGaussPoints=4` points writing 6 components each = **24 doubles into a 6-double static heap block**. `Vector::operator()` is bounds-checked only under `_G3DEBUG`, so release builds silently wrote 18 doubles past the end on *every recorder step* → heap corruption / segfault when recording tet10 element fields (`Brick` gets this right with `stresses(48)`; `setResponse` here already advertises `Vector(6*4)`). Fix `stresses(6*NumGaussPoints)`. Secondary: correct sizing also stops `Information::setVector` from reallocating the response Vector down to 6 (`Vector::operator=` resizes on mismatch), which desynchronised `ElementRecorder`'s column layout even when it did not crash. Both are **upstream candidates** (identical code in `OpenSees/OpenSees` master) — see campaign row 0.0. | [#692](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/692) |

| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno` **upstream bug fix**: every SECOND consecutive `eigen` in openseespy with NO analysis object failed with "no EigenSOE has been set". Mechanism: `OpenSeesCommands::eigen` builds an EPHEMERAL `DirectIntegrationAnalysis` per call and deletes it after the solve (no-op dtor — the cached `theEigenSOE` survives); the next call builds a fresh analysis whose internal EigenSOE is null, but a same-classTag cached SOE leaves `eigenSOEUpdated=false`, so `setEigenSOE` is never re-invoked. Fix: attach also when `newanalysis` is true (`(eigenSOEUpdated \|\| newanalysis)`). Classic Tcl re-attaches and never had the bug. Upstream `OpenSees/OpenSees` master carries the same defect — the ephemeral-analysis build, the `newanalysis` flag, and the `eigenSOEUpdated`-gated attach block all match upstream — so it is an **upstream candidate**, but as a **re-port not a cherry-pick**: the fork's surrounding lines carry the Ladruno FEAST/CMS `providedEigenSOE` seam (ADR-43) that upstream lacks, so the patch will not apply verbatim. Measured verdict: implicit setSize is LINEAR (N^1.05 over the 0.25/0.5 M Newmark+Mumps rungs); the rowfill global-eq probe loop (O(neq_global × log V_local)/rank, np-invariant) is flagged in the ADR for cluster-scale re-measurement. | ADR-74 implicit PR |

| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/pardiso/PARDISOGenLinSolver.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1`: **factorization reuse**. Stock ran PARDISO phases 11→22→33→−1 on EVERY solve with `pt[]`/`iparm` as function locals (redoing the METIS reorder *and* the numeric factorization per solve, then freeing them; `iparm` leaked). Now: handle/control array are members; phase 11 once per sparsity pattern (`setSize`), phase 22 only when `A` changed (gated on the SOE `factored` flag, mirroring `MumpsSolver` job=5/job=3), phase 33 per call, phase −1 once in the dtor. Also `iparm[2]=1`→0 (does not pin threads; `MKL_NUM_THREADS` does), `iparm[18]`→0 (Intel documents −1 as increasing reordering time; never printed at `msglvl=0`), MKL error-code decoding. Adversarial review fixed 3 defects: dtor use-after-free (`~LinearSOE` deletes the solver *after* the SOE freed its arrays ⇒ cache `cachedN`, pass dummies), a virgin-handle path (`needsSymbolic \|\| !init`), and the iparm[18] cost. Compile-verified; not yet built/registered. | [#622](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/622) |
| `CMakeLists.txt`, `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/CMakeLists.txt`, `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/pardiso/CMakeLists.txt` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1b`: build+link MKL PARDISO for the **serial/desktop** targets. `add_subdirectory(pardiso)` gated on `MKL_FOUND` (non-MKL builds, e.g. Zone-A Ubuntu, never compile it and simply have no `system Pardiso`); new `PARDISO_FLAG`/`PARDISO_MKL_LIBRARIES` linked to `OpenSees` + `OpenSeesPy` only. **Threading layer is the crux:** parallel targets keep `mkl_sequential` (correct — their parallelism is MPI, and it is what ScaLAPACK/MUMPS use) while serial takes `mkl_intel_thread` + `libiomp5md`, because desktop PARDISO's entire value is shared-memory threading. Mixing layers in one process is unsupported by MKL, so they are kept on disjoint targets — **the MP/MUMPS lane is untouched**. | [#623](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/623) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1b`: register `system Pardiso` (and `PARDISO`) + the `OPS_PARDISOGenLinSolver()` factory, under `#ifdef _PARDISO`. Note the factory uses a LOCAL SOE pointer: `theSOE` is a **member of `OpenSeesCommands`** (`OpenSeesCommands.h:178`), not a file-scope global, so a free factory cannot assign it. | `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/mumps/MumpsParallelSolver.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P2`: expose MUMPS **Block Low-Rank** — new `icntl35`/`cntl7` members + ctor params (default 0/0.0 ⇒ byte-identical to stock). `ICNTL(35)`/`CNTL(7)` are applied at BOTH the analysis (job=1) and factorization paths (MUMPS uses ICNTL(35) when planning the assembly tree, so setting it only at factor time under-delivers). **`sendSelf`/`recvSelf` extended 2→3 ints + a `Vector(1)` for the double**: without shipping `icntl35`, rank 0 would factor BLR while every subordinate factored full-rank — an inconsistent distributed factorization that would produce wrong answers rather than an error. | [#625](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/625) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P2`: `system Mumps -BLR <eps>` (sets ICNTL35=1 + CNTL7=eps) plus raw `-ICNTL35`/`-CNTL7` escape hatches. BLR is an **approximate** factorization — opt-in, off by default, and must stay off byte-identical/oracle lanes. | [#625](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/625) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/pardiso/PARDISOGenLinSOE.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1d`: **symmetric upper-triangle half-storage.** New `matType` member + ctor overload (0 unsym full CSR / 1 SPD / 2 symmetric general, numbering deliberately matching `system Mumps -matrixType`). `setSize` halves the off-diagonal nnz — `(nnz−n)/2+n`, exact because `Graph::addEdge` is strictly two-sided (it `exit(0)`s otherwise) — and skips adjacency entries `<= vertexTag`; the diagonal is placed first and is smaller than every kept entry, so the existing sorted insertion still yields the ASCENDING column order PARDISO requires. `addA` filters to `col >= row` via a `!halfStore ||` short-circuit rather than MumpsSOE's duplicated loop nest, so `matType 0` stays byte-identical. **Documented hazard:** on a genuinely unsymmetric tangent only the upper half is read — no averaging, no detection — hence the default stays 0 (ADR-75 §5.2-bis). Measured: stored nnz -49.3%, peak memory -41.8%. **Adversarial review** added: a hard bound on the fill (the nnz count is a PREDICTION; the failure mode if the graph assumption breaks is a silent heap overflow), a post-fill check that every CSR row is strictly ascending and carries its diagonal (PARDISO can return a plausible WRONG answer otherwise; note the diagonal is only FIRST when half-storing, so the test is presence not position), an `addA` symmetry detector that reports the discarded-lower-triangle hazard instead of only documenting it (budgeted to the first 3 tangent assemblies via `zeroA`, so the cost is independent of model size), and a real OOM path (`std::nothrow` + `return -1`; the stock check was DEAD CODE since plain `new` throws, `Asize` clobbered the recovery, and `delete[]` ran before the allocation). | [#630](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/630) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/pardiso/PARDISOGenLinSolver.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1d`: `mtype` is no longer hardcoded 11 — it is **derived** from the SOE's `matType` at the symbolic phase (11 / 2 / −2) with deliberately **no setter**, which makes a storage-vs-factorization-mode mismatch unrepresentable; it persists as a member for the dtor's phase −1. Pivoting/scaling now branch on it (`iparm[9]` 13→8, `iparm[10]`/`iparm[12]` 1→0, `iparm[20]=1` Bunch-Kaufman): these are Intel's documented **per-mtype defaults**, not a shared default. *(Corrected in P1e: this row previously claimed MKL applies scaling/matching as an unsymmetric permutation for ±2 and that enabling them is the classic "symmetric PARDISO returns garbage" cause. That is NOT Intel's position — Intel explicitly supports `iparm[10]=1`+`iparm[12]=1` for mtype −2 and RECOMMENDS it for highly indefinite symmetric systems, saddle-point structures in particular. The source comment was fixed by the P1d adversarial review in `8a07e7642`; this ledger row was missed.)* Also **`-stats`** (`setStats`): dumps `iparm[14]/[15]/[16]` + total peak = `max(iparm[14], iparm[15]+iparm[16])` and factor nnz (`iparm[17]=-1`, which unlike `iparm[18]` Intel does not document as slowing the reorder), ONCE per sparsity pattern; mirrors the MUMPS `-stats` of P2b. Error `-4` now names the SPD-mis-selection case. | [#630](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/630) |
| `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1d`: `system Pardiso [-matrixType 0\|1\|2] [-symmetric] [-spd] [-stats]` in the **TCL** interpreter + the `#ifdef _PARDISO` includes. **P1b gap:** the verb had been registered only in `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp`, so `system Pardiso` worked from OpenSeesPy but not from `OpenSees.exe` — this Tcl `system` chain is a completely separate if-ladder. Parity proven by `phase1/p1d_tcl_smoke.tcl` (all 6 spellings identical to 12 digits). | [#630](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/630) |
| `CMakeLists.txt` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1d`: `target_compile_definitions(OpenSees PRIVATE ${PARDISO_FLAG})` + the pardiso include dir, so the Tcl branch above compiles. **Serial exe only** — `OpenSeesSP/MP` compile the same `commands.cpp` but link the *sequential* MKL layer, and mixing threaded/sequential MKL in one process is unsupported (the P1b disjoint-target rule). Cluster runs use `system Mumps`. | [#630](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/630) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1d`: `-matrixType`/`-symmetric`/`-spd`/`-stats` parsing in `OPS_PARDISOGenLinSolver()`, loop bound `> 0` from the start (the P2b bare-flag lesson applied pre-emptively). **A parse failure must NOT `return 0`:** that leaves `theSOE` null and OpenSees falls back to the default **`ProfileSPDLinSOE`** with only a warning — catastrophically slow on a 3D solid mesh, so the run looks merely slow rather than wrong. Found the hard way: openseespy `system('Pardiso','-matrixType','2')` with a STRING value fails `OPS_GetIntInput` and sent a Lane-B bench into ProfileSPD for 25 minutes. It now degrades to unsymmetric, which is what the warning always claimed. (`system Mumps` still carries the same self-contradictory return-0-after-"assumed" path — flagged, not changed here.) | [#630](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/630) |
| `Ladruno_scripts/build.bat` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1d`: add **`mkl_avx10`** to `MKL_RUNTIME_DLLS`. oneMKL 2026.1 ships `mkl_avx10.3.dll` as a NEW CPU-dispatch kernel alongside `mkl_avx512`/`mkl_mc3`; absent on 2025 (the glob just matches nothing), but missing on AVX10 hardware MKL would fail to dispatch or silently fall back to a slower kernel. Rides on the base-name globbing that [#627](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/627) introduced concurrently — P1d hit the same MKL `.2.dll`→`.3.dll` rename independently (build succeeded, then `ImportError: DLL load failed while importing opensees`) and its duplicate fix was dropped in favour of #627's, which also purges stale DLLs. See `BUILD_GOTCHAS.md §9`. | [#630](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/630) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/mumps/MumpsParallelSolver.{h,cpp}`, `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P2b`: `system Mumps -stats` dumps MUMPS `INFOG(9)/(21)/(22)`, `RINFOG(3)` and (under BLR) `RINFOG(14)/(15)` after each numeric factorization on rank 0 — BLR's whole justification is factor MEMORY, previously invisible from OpenSees. Handles MUMPS's negative-INFOG = −value×10⁶ convention; BLR fields printed raw + index-labelled rather than reinterpreted. **Also widened the `system Mumps` option loop from `> 1` to `> 0`:** its "every option takes a value" premise (an ADR-43 fix) broke for the bare `-stats` flag — a trailing `-stats` was silently ignored, which would have produced a false "BLR shows no stats" reading. | [#626](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/626) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/pardiso/PARDISOGenLinSolver.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1e`: **`-krylov <L>` — factorization-preconditioned CGS (`iparm[3] = 10*L+K`).** The P1 `factored` gate can only skip work when A is UNCHANGED, so it pays *nothing* under full Newton; `iparm[3]` is the complementary axis — it reuses the retained L/U as a **preconditioner** for a tangent that HAS changed. New `setKrylov(digits)` + `krylovL`/`krylovK` members; K is derived at the symbolic phase from `mtype` (1 = LU-CGS for 11, 2 = LL^T-CG for 2) and **refused with a warning for mtype −2** — Intel documents K=2 for symmetric *positive definite* only, so the symmetric-indefinite path (the right one for a softening tangent) cannot use this lever at all. Three non-obvious pieces: (1) **phase 23 is mandatory** — Intel documents the automatic direct fallback ("the factorization … is automatically recomputed in cases where the Krylov Subspace iteration failed") for phase 23 ONLY; under phase 33 the same failure is just `error=-4`. (2) **A CGS *win* leaves the stored factors one tangent behind**, so the new `factorsCurrent` flag forbids the phase-33 shortcut afterwards — without it a second solve against the same A silently answers with the PREVIOUS tangent (silent-wrong-answer, not a crash); `haveFactors` is tracked separately (is there a preconditioner at all). (3) `iparm[19]` is decoded (`-it_cgs*10 - cgs_error`), and **`cgs_error 5` is reported once even without `-stats`** — it is Intel's own "this flag is not worth it on this matrix", whose only other symptom is a run that is quietly slower. Perturbed-pivot check hoisted to `ops_pardiso_perturbed()` since the phase-23 path can also end in a factorization. Off by default ⇒ byte-identical to P1d when `-krylov` is absent. **Measured (Lane B, full Newton, 15 steps): 1 thread 1.53× @11.5k DOF / 2.03× @26.5k; 4 threads 1.24× / 1.30×** — the win is real but threading erodes it (factorization parallelizes, triangular solves do not), so the 4-thread production number is the honest headline. | [#633](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/633) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1e`: `system Pardiso -krylov <digits>` parsing in `OPS_PARDISOGenLinSolver()` + `setKrylov()` call. Value-taking (not a bare flag): the useful L is model-dependent. Parse failure **degrades to 0 (direct)** rather than returning 0, per the P1d rule — returning 0 here leaves `theSOE` null and OpenSees silently falls back to `ProfileSPDLinSOE`. | [#633](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/633) |
| `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1e`: the same `-krylov <digits>` option in the **TCL** `system Pardiso` branch, keeping the two interpreters in step (the P1b/P1d lesson: the Tcl `system` chain is a separate if-ladder and drifts silently). Missing value warns and continues; a non-int is `TCL_ERROR`, matching `-matrixType`. | [#633](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/633) |
| `SRC/material/nD/TclModelBuilderNDMaterialCommand.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: **Tcl parity for the fork's nD materials.** 10 `OPS_*` factories / **15 names** (with every alias) added to the Tcl `nDMaterial` strcmp ladder + their `extern` decls: `LogStrain`/`LogStrainNDMaterial`, `LogStrain2D`, `LadrunoCohesiveHingeBiaxial`, `LadrunoJ2`, `LadrunoJ2Finite`, `InitDefGrad`/`InitDefGradNDMaterial`/`StagedDefGrad`/`StagedDefGradNDMaterial`, `StagedStrain`/`StagedStrainNDMaterial`, `LadrunoRCConcrete`, `LadrunoRCFiniteStrain`, `LadrunoConcrete3D`. **They were registered ONLY in the openseespy map** (`SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesNDMaterialCommands.cpp`), and this Tcl command is a separate hand-written ladder with **no generic `OPS_*` fallback**, so `nDMaterial LadrunoJ2 ...` failed from every `.tcl` deck while the identical call worked from Python. The **elements** were wired long ago (dispatch table at `SRC/element/TclElementCommands.cpp:596`), which is exactly why the materials gap was easy to miss. Each branch mirrors the surrounding convention (`OPS_ResetInput()` has already primed the `OPS_Get*` stream; null return ⇒ `TCL_ERROR`); names/aliases mirror the Python map one-for-one so the surfaces cannot drift again. **Purely additive** — no existing branch touched, so every pre-existing deck parses identically. Verified: `cl /Zs` on the exact ninja `OPS_InterpTcl` flags PASSES with a **negative control** failing at the injected line; a real **link** on the cluster build; and **runtime** — `nDMaterial LadrunoJ2` + `element LadrunoBrick` converge from a `.tcl` deck under `OpenSeesMP`. Motivation: the gap had forced ADR-75b's G-L3 gate onto a proxy material. | ADR-75b G-L3 follow-up [#658](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/658) |
| `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P2h`: **`system Mumps` Tcl ladder — wire the P2/P2b options that existed only in Python, and stop the silent drop.** `-BLR <eps>`, `-stats`/`-mumpsStats`, `-ICNTL35 <n>`, `-CNTL7 <x>` added, and the ctor call changed from `MumpsParallelSolver(icntl7, icntl14)` to the 5-arg form on **both** parallel arms. **Why it mattered more than the P1d/P1e cases:** `OpenSeesMP` is Tcl-driven, so the cluster — *the only place MUMPS runs* — could not reach BLR at all, and the ladder's `else currentArg++` arm dropped unknown options **silently**, so `system Mumps -BLR 1e-8 -stats` produced a plain full-rank solve with no stats. That does not fail; it reads as "BLR does not move `INFOG(21)`" — a **false negative that would have been recorded as the ADR-75 item-1 measurement**. Also fixes a latent **read past the end of `argv`** (every value-taking option read `argv[currentArg+1]` with no bounds check, so a trailing `-ICNTL14` overran) and replaces the silent skip with a warning. Unknown options stay **non-fatal** (decks may carry options a given build lacks) but now leave a mark. Existing spellings byte-identical: BLR/stats default off ⇒ the 5-arg ctor reproduces the 2-arg defaults exactly. Names/defaults mirror `OpenSeesCommands.cpp` so the two ladders cannot drift. `-matrixType` was already present here and is **untouched**. **Blast radius checked, not assumed:** every `system Mumps` invocation in the tree that goes through *this* ladder (`EXAMPLES/**.tcl`, `tests/ladruno_cms_physical_smoke.tcl`) passes **no options at all**, so the new warning fires on nothing; every options-bearing caller is Python and uses the other ladder. **Residual drift, deliberately left and recorded rather than hidden:** `-commSplit` (ADR-43 P3a) is still Python-only — it is collective (`MPI_Comm_split`, every rank must call it) and has no Tcl gate, so wiring it blind was out of scope; a Tcl deck passing it now gets a warning instead of silence, which is the point. Serial and SP arms gain explicit "not supported / not honoured" warnings rather than dropping the option (checked where we *depend* on it: the serial `MumpsSolver` ctor carries no BLR/stats parameters, and the SP `MumpsParallelSOE` ctor takes no `matType`). **Verified by compiling all three `#ifdef` arms** (`_PARALLEL_INTERPRETERS`, `_PARALLEL_PROCESSING`, serial) with `cl /Zs` under the exact ninja `OpenSeesMP` defines/includes, with a negative control (injected error inside the new `-BLR` branch ⇒ FAIL at that line) proving the check is not vacuous. **NOT executed** — no Tcl+MUMPS binary was reachable this session (the cluster rebuild was deferred by the owner), so the runtime parity smoke (the `p1d_tcl_smoke.tcl` analogue) is owed at the item-1 cluster run. | ADR-75 P2h [#640](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/640) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/pardiso/PARDISOGenLinSOE.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1f`: **`addA` scatter — linear scan → binary search.** The stock inner loop rescanned the whole CSR row for every one of `idSize²` element entries, i.e. `O(idSize² × rowlen)`; for a 3D brick that is 24×24 lookups each walking a ~81-entry row. ADR-75b's L3-0 profile measured it at **1699 ms of an ~11.9 s step, 1.28× slower than UmfPack's** equivalent scatter, and it only grew in relative terms as P1a/P1d/P1e made the *solve* cheaper. New `ops_pardiso_findCol()` binary search; legal because the ascending-CSR invariant is **enforced**, not assumed — `setSize` already rejects the matrix (`size = 0`, `return -1`) if any row's columns are not strictly ascending. **Measured (Lane B, 4 threads, interleaved A/B against a snapshot of the pre-fix binary): 1.098× at 26.5k DOF, `-matrixType 1` 1.029×.** Exactness verified at `MKL_NUM_THREADS=1`, where old and new agree **bit-for-bit over 10 runs each** (the 4-thread comparison is useless for this — see the determinism quirk). Also adds a **free miss detector**: the `k >= 0` test already existed, so `else missing = 1` costs a never-taken branch and turns "an element entry has no CSR slot ⇒ its stiffness is silently discarded" from undetectable into a once-per-SOE warning; the old linear scan simply fell off the end of the row. | [#636](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/636) |
| `SRC/analysis/algorithm/equiSolnAlgo/ModifiedNewton.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-76 (R4)`: `OPS_ModifiedNewton()` parser `if (OPS_GetNumRemainingInputArgs() > 0)` → `while (...)`. Upstream read exactly ONE option string into an `else if` chain, so `algorithm ModifiedNewton -initial -factoronce` silently honoured `-initial` and dropped the rest — yet that pair is the natural spelling of "initial stiffness, assembled and factorized once", for a static analysis arguably the cheapest robust algorithm the framework offers. Matches the `while` form `OPS_NewtonRaphsonAlgorithm()` already uses. **Adversarial review then found four defects in that one-line cut, all fixed in the same PR:** (1) `-hall`'s `return 0` on a failed factor read became reachable from non-first positions -- and openseespy's `OPS_Algorithm` DID `if (theAlgo != 0) setAlgorithm` then report SUCCESS, so a null silently left the PREVIOUS algorithm in force with no Python exception; now warns and keeps the 0.1/0.9 defaults, per the ADR-75 P1d degrade-don't-return-null rule — AND the choke point itself now errors on null (see the `OpenSeesCommands.cpp` ADR-76 row below). (2) `-factorOnce` (camelCase -- what `Linear.cpp:67`, `ExpressNewton.cpp:78`, both `commands.cpp` sites and LEDGER_quirks itself use) was STILL silently dropped by the fix meant to stop options being dropped; now accepted, with `-Initial`/`-Secant` for NewtonRaphson parity. (3) `-secant`/`-initial` now reset `iFactor`/`cFactor` as `OPS_NewtonRaphsonAlgorithm()` does -- unnecessary under an `if`, required once a later option can override an earlier one. (4) unknown tokens now warn instead of vanishing, which also makes the `-hall` arity quirk self-diagnosing. Left alone: the `== 2` gate itself, since NewtonRaphson carries the identical one and fixing only this parser would leave two parsers for the same flag disagreeing. **Not behaviour-preserving in general:** decks passing 2+ recognised options now get the LAST one to set a field, not the first -- a silent results change, though no in-tree deck passes more than one option. Single choke point for BOTH interpreters (Tcl `specifyAlgorithm` `commands.cpp:4467` + openseespy `OpenSeesCommands.cpp:2017`); `SRC/runtime/commands/analysis/algorithm.cpp` parses `algorithm` a third time and drops `factorOnce` entirely, but that tree is in no `add_subdirectory()` and is dead in this build — left untouched. Single-option decks byte-identical. | [#642](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/642) |
| `SRC/analysis/algorithm/equiSolnAlgo/NewtonRaphson.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-76 (R1)`: **comment only, zero behaviour change.** Block note above the iteration `do`-loop recording that `formTangent` sits INSIDE it, so `algorithm Newton -initial` re-assembles AND re-factorizes every iteration (measured 1.61× `ModifiedNewton -initial` at 39k DOF on PARDISO for an identical answer in an identical iteration count); pointing users at `ModifiedNewton -initial` as the cheap spelling of the same algorithm **where the initial tangent is genuinely state-independent** (not corot3d / contact / -damping / betaK!=0); and recording WHY the obvious skip is not applied here — `addKiToTang()` reaches `Element::getInitialStiff()`, which is configuration-dependent for `CorotCrdTransf3d` and `LadrunoContactFE`, so invariance is an element property, not an integrator-family one. See [[76_ladruno_tangent_reuse_adr]] + [[LEDGER_quirks]]. | [#642](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/642) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/bandGEN/BandGenLinLapackSolver.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (ADR-76 audit spin-off): **`return -info+1;` → `return -info;`** — a singular matrix reported SUCCESS. C parses `-info+1` as `(-info)+1`, and LAPACK sets `info = i` when `U(i,i)` is exactly zero, so `info == 1` — a first-pivot singularity, i.e. exactly what an all-zero assembled `A` produces — returned **0**. Not cosmetic: the solver copies `B` into `X` *before* calling LAPACK (`:116-118`), DGBSV does not compute `X` when `info > 0`, and the caller gates on `theSOE->solve() < 0` — so the algorithm consumed the **load vector** as a displacement increment. `theSOE->factored = true` (`:166` post-fix) is also never reached on the error path, so the flag stays false and every later iteration silently repeats the same non-solve. **Measured before the fix** (3 free DOF, `Elastic` E=0, load 7.0, `algorithm Linear`): `analyze` returned **0** with `nodeDisp == 7.0`. `-info` is always negative in this branch and keeps the failing pivot index in the magnitude; it is the idiom already used at `SymBandEigenSolver.cpp:228` / `FullGenEigenSolver.cpp:188`. No caller reads the magnitude — no comparison against a specific value exists anywhere in `SRC/`, so the change is safe. **Correction (fact-check):** an earlier wording said "every call site gates on `< 0`"; that is FALSE. Dozens of call sites ignore the return code entirely (`ArcLength.cpp:244,1053,1064`, `DisplacementControl.cpp:321,1092,1102`, `LadrunoArcLength.cpp:379`, `Newmark.cpp:1047`, `SensitivityAlgorithm.cpp:133`, the accelerator family, ...) — which means the BUG was worse than documented: on an arc-length or displacement-control step the singular-solve return was never inspected at all. Regression: `Ladruno_implementation/lapack_singular_regression/`. Behaviour change to state: decks that currently limp past a singular step will now fail loudly — which is the point. | [#642](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/642) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/fullGEN/FullGenLinLapackSolver.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (ADR-76 audit spin-off): identical `return -info+1;` → `return -info;` defect and fix; see the `BandGenLinLapackSolver.cpp` row for the analysis. Measured pre-fix: `analyze` == 0, `nodeDisp` == the applied load. | [#642](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/642) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/bandSPD/BandSPDLinLapackSolver.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (ADR-76 audit spin-off): identical `return -info+1;` → `return -info;` defect and fix; see the `BandGenLinLapackSolver.cpp` row. **Extra trap recorded in the source comment:** this file's own 1x1 special-case guard (`:98`) intercepts a single-DOF singular system before the LAPACK call, so a 1-DOF repro reports this solver as CLEAN — the first version of our probe did exactly that. Any regression here must use n >= 2. | [#642](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/642) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-76 (adversarial review)`: `OPS_Algorithm()` now returns **−1 on a null factory result** (`:2060`) instead of falling through to `return 0` — pre-fix, openseespy reported SUCCESS while silently leaving the PREVIOUS algorithm in force, a wrong-answer path with no Python exception. Every algorithm factory can return null on a bad option (`OPS_ModifiedNewton`, `OPS_NewtonRaphsonAlgorithm`, `OPS_ExpressNewton`, …), so fixing it at this choke point closes the hole for all of them at once. Restores parity with classic Tcl, which always errored on null (`commands.cpp:4469`). Behaviour change: a bad `algorithm` call now raises in Python instead of silently keeping the old algorithm — which is the point. | [#642](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/642) |
| `SRC/analysis/algorithm/equiSolnAlgo/Linear.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (ADR-76): **profiler `formTangent` scope moved INSIDE the `factorOnce != 2` guard.** It used to wrap the guard, so a `Linear -factorOnce` profile counted one `formTangent` per step even when the latch skipped the assembly — every such profile ever taken overstated assemblies (call count == nSteps regardless). `ModifiedNewton::solveCurrentStep` had it right (scope inside the guard) and is the reference pattern. Profiling-only: zero behaviour change to the analysis; profiles of algorithms without `-factorOnce` are unaffected (the guard is always taken). | [#642](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/642) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/eigenSOE/ArpackSOE.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno ADR-1000 §31.5 (apeGmsh ADR 0077 F1)`: public `setProcessID`/`setChannels` (same contract as `MumpsParallelSOE`'s). The class always HAD the P0-star parallel machinery — the `myMv` M*v merge, the `checkSameInt` ido lockstep guard, the collective global-size `setSize` — but its only wiring path was SP's `sendSelf`/`recvSelf` object shipping, which `_PARALLEL_INTERPRETERS` never invokes; under MP `processID` stayed −1 and every collective was dormant (this is the *runtime* form of the F1 defect ADR 0077 described as `#ifdef`-gated — same consequence). The destructor also gains the frees for the wiring bookkeeping it never had (`theChannels`/`localCol`/`sizeLocal` — review finding: vanilla `~ArpackSOE` freed only `M`, so `recvSelf`-wired SP SOEs leaked these too). Behaviorally additive; serial and SP unchanged (the setters compile in everywhere but are called only under `_PARALLEL_INTERPRETERS`). | [#668](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/668) |
| `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-1000 §31.5 (apeGmsh ADR 0077 F1)`: MP `eigen` now wires the bare `ArpackSOE` (`eigenAnalysis()`, creation path AND the reuse-on-classTag-match path) with `setProcessID(OPS_rank)`/`setChannels`, **gated on the analysis LinearSOE being `LinSOE_TAGS_MumpsParallelSOE`** — with a serial system each rank already holds the full replicated problem and the merge would sum np copies of M*v (the reuse path re-gates on every call and drops back to `setProcessID(-1)` if the deck switched to a serial system). Makes plain `eigen` over a **partitioned** deck + `system Mumps` under `OpenSeesMP.exe` a *correct distributed* eigensolve: replicated ARPACK outer loop, distributed dmumps `(K−σM)` factor+solve, P0-star-merged M*v — the same replicated-outer/distributed-inner shape as FEAST L3, and the "competitor at home" reference (b) the ADR-1000 §31.4 Esmeralda campaign requires. Negative control (pre-fix exe, 2-rank partitioned smoke): rank 0 returned an EMPTY spectrum, rank 1 deadlocked. Post-fix `Ladruno_scripts/verify_arpack_mp_mumps.tcl` (analytic fixed-free-chain oracle + mode-1 shape-ratio check): serial 5.9e-16 / np=2 1.3e-15 / np=4 8.3e-16 λ rel err, shapes ≤2.2e-15, values identical across ranks; FEAST resmoke unchanged. **Known scope holes, recorded not closed** (ADR-1000 §34.4): the gate covers only `MumpsParallelSOE`, so `system ParallelProfileSPD` (`DistributedProfileSPDLinSOE`) and `system MPIDiagonal` keep the old unwired-and-broken `eigen` path — and the reuse `else` branch's "restores serial semantics" is false for those two (they are partitioned, not replicated); `modalDamping` becomes *reachable* on a partitioned MP deck where its modal projection is a rank-local partial sum (silently np-dependent damping); nodal mass double-defined on a shared boundary node is double-counted by the merge with no detection (owner discipline is deck convention only); and the same F1 defect remains in openseespy/PyMP (`OpenSeesCommands.cpp:376` builds an equally bare `ArpackSOE`). `#ifdef _PARALLEL_INTERPRETERS` only — serial `OpenSees.exe` unchanged. | [#668](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/668) |
| `SRC/analysis/integrator/GeneralizedAlpha.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-77 (C0-6)`: **`setResponse(*Ualpha,*Ualphadot,*Udotdot)` → `setResponse(*Ualpha,*Ualphadot,*Ualphadotdot)`** in `update()` (`:405`). `Ualphadotdot` was computed on the immediately preceding line and then **discarded**, so the inertia term was evaluated at `t+deltaT` instead of `t+alphaM*deltaT` on **every Newton iteration** — the separate `alphaM` weighting of the ACCELERATION is the one thing distinguishing generalized-alpha from HHT, so the class was not the scheme it claims to be. `newStep()` already set it correctly (`setAccel(*Ualphadotdot)`, `:193`); `update()` then overwrote it, so only the predictor was right. **The identical-looking line in `HHT.cpp:394` is CORRECT and must not be "fixed"** — HHT weights only stiffness/damping and its inertia genuinely belongs at `t+deltaT`. Diagnosis confirmed by **prediction**, not inspection: the defect must vanish exactly at `alphaM==1.0` (where `Ualphadotdot == Udotdot` identically), and it does — pre-fix L2 error vs a fine reference was 2.16e-2 at `alphaM=1.0` (matching HHT(0.8)'s 2.07e-2) but 1.01e-1 / 7.68e-1 / 2.01e+2 at `alphaM=` 0.80 / 0.65 / 0.50, worsening monotonically with distance from 1.0; **pre-fix observed convergence order was NEGATIVE** (−1.34 / −4.41 / −6.71 — error *growing* as `dt` shrinks, which no consistent scheme does). **`LadrunoGeneralizedAlpha` (ADR-52 W3-I2) subclasses this and does not override `update()`, so it inherited the defect** — measured byte-identical to vanilla before and after; a fork feature shipped on a broken base. Behaviour change to state: **`alphaM==1.0` (rho_inf=0.5) is bit-identical by construction** (regression-guarded); every other `alphaM` changes, including the undamped `rho_inf=1` default a user reaches for first — which is the point. ADR-49a calls GeneralizedAlpha the "gold standard"; that row needs a caveat for every release before this fix. Acceptance test: `Ladruno_files/testbed/perf/phase77/c06_genalpha_verify.py` (`before`/`after`). See [[77_ladruno_implicit_transient_adr]] / [[77a_c0_t0_results_2026-07-26]]. | [#650](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/650) |
| `SRC/analysis/integrator/TRBDF2.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-77 (C0-1 + C0-3)`: **C0-1** — `newStep()` checked `U == 0` but never `deltaT`, then divided: `c2 = 2.0/deltaT`, `c3 = 4.0/(deltaT*deltaT)`. `analyze(n, 0.0)` built inf/nan constants and an inf/nan tangent with **no diagnostic** — the run completed and reported numbers. Guard added with the same wording/return code (`-2`) as `HHT::newStep`; every `deltaT > 0` takes the identical path. **Verified post-fix:** `analyze(1, 0.0)` now returns `rc=-2` with `TRBDF2::newStep() - error in variable / dT = 0`. **C0-3** — comment only, **no behaviour change**: this ADR's C0 audit initially mis-filed `sendSelf`/`recvSelf` as stub-shaped defects alongside the contact handler's P1a stubs. **That was a false positive and is withdrawn** — TRBDF2 is constructed parameterless (`new TRBDF2()`), so `return 0` is *correct*. TRBDF3/Houbolt already carried a `// nothing to send` note and TRBDF2 did not, which is the only reason it looked different; the note is now there so the next reader does not repeat the mistake. | [#650](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/650) |
| `SRC/analysis/integrator/TRBDF3.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-77 (C0-1)`: same missing `deltaT` guard as the `TRBDF2.cpp` row — `c2`/`c3` divide by `deltaT` and `deltaT^2` on all three legs, so `analyze(n, 0.0)` produced an inf/nan tangent silently. Guard added, `HHT::newStep` wording, return `-2`. Verified: now `rc=-2`. (Its `sendSelf`/`recvSelf` were *also* audited as stubs and are *also* a withdrawn false positive — parameterless class, and it already documents `// nothing to send`.) | [#650](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/650) |
| `SRC/analysis/integrator/Houbolt.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-77 (C0-1)`: same missing `deltaT` guard — see the `TRBDF2.cpp` row. Guard added, return `-2`. Verified: now `rc=-2`. | [#650](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/650) |
| `SRC/analysis/integrator/BackwardEuler.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-77 (C0-1 + C0-2)`: **C0-1** missing `deltaT` guard (see `TRBDF2.cpp` row); verified `rc=-2`. **C0-2 — the worst finding of the C0 audit: a silent wrong answer.** `formEleTangent` had no `HALL_TANGENT` branch, so it called `zeroTangent()` and then added **nothing** — `algorithm Newton -hall` assembled an **ALL-ZERO element tangent** and the analysis *completed*. `HALL_TANGENT` is reachable from `NewtonRaphson -hall`, `ModifiedNewton -hall`, `NewtonHallM` **and** `ExpressNewton`, so this is not an exotic path. Branch transposed from `Newmark::formEleTangent` (`c1` is the stiffness coefficient here too; `iFactor`/`cFactor` are protected members of `IncrementalIntegrator`), plus the `else { unknown FLAG }` diagnostic Newmark has. `CURRENT_TANGENT`/`INITIAL_TANGENT` paths untouched. **Verified post-fix:** `BackwardEuler -hall` now converges to `uz = -1.932969365069346e-02`, exactly its own non-`-hall` answer (as it must when `Kt ≈ Ki`, since `cFactor·Kt + iFactor·Ki → K` for `0.2 + 0.8 = 1`). | [#650](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/650) |
| `SRC/analysis/integrator/Newmark1.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-77 (C0-2)`: missing `HALL_TANGENT` branch — identical silent all-zero tangent as the `BackwardEuler.cpp` row. Branch + `unknown FLAG` diagnostic added. **Verified:** `Newmark1 -hall` now matches plain Newmark to **3.5e-16** relative. | [#650](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/650) |
| `SRC/analysis/integrator/Collocation.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-77 (C0-2)`: missing `HALL_TANGENT` branch, and **arguably worse than the zero-tangent case**: Collocation adds `C` and `M` *outside* the if/else chain, so `-hall` assembled `c2·C + c3·M` with **no stiffness at all** — a tangent that looks populated. Branch added inside the chain; the C/M adds deliberately left outside so `CURRENT`/`INITIAL` behaviour is byte-for-byte unchanged. **Verified:** `Collocation -hall` now matches plain Newmark to **4.7e-13** relative. | [#650](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/650) |
| `SRC/analysis/integrator/Newmark.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-77 (C0-4)`: removed `static bool converged = false;` (was `:56`) — a **file-scope mutable global shared by every Newmark instance in the process**. It was written in `newStep()` (`true`) and `revertToLastStep()` (`false`), and its **only read sat inside a commented-out debug block** that dumped `U` to `"Newmark.dat"`. Dead state carrying a live re-entrancy hazard for zero benefit — exactly the static-idiom class [[75b_ladruno_threaded_assembly_adr]] §5.4 catalogued as a threaded-assembly blocker. Both writes and the commented-out reader removed with it. Removing state nothing reads cannot change results; confirmed bit-identical by the T3 regression sweep. | [#650](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/650) |
| `SRC/analysis/algorithm/equiSolnAlgo/KrylovNewton.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-77 (C0-5)`: **P3 profiler scopes — profiling-only, zero behaviour change** (`OPS_PROFILE_SCOPE` is an RAII timer). Only `Linear`, `ModifiedNewton` and `NewtonRaphson` carried these, so **8 of 11 `EquiSolnAlgo` implementations produced a step the profiler could not decompose**: the leaf scopes still appeared (they live in the integrator, not the algorithm) but the `formTangent`/`formUnbalance`/`linearSolve`/`update`/`convTest` wrappers did not, so solve time landed in the unattributed remainder. Scopes and wrapping shape copied verbatim from `NewtonRaphson.cpp`. Applied by brace-matching script rather than by hand (8 files x ~5 sites); the compiler is the balance check. **This is the file that motivated C0-5:** `KrylovNewton` is the best-performing algorithm in the entire ADR-77 study (T1's winner at every nonlinearity level, and T3's fixed algorithm) and was the one the profiler could not decompose — its solve time fell into the unattributed remainder of `solveCurrentStep`, which an earlier T1 report rendered as a fabricated `0.0%`. **Verified post-fix:** the unattributed remainder dropped from ~28.6% to **0.2%**, with `formTangent` 27.2% / `formUnbalance` 22.6% / `linearSolve` 19.0% / `update` 12.5% now resolved. Known cosmetic limitation: because this file has two same-named `formTangent` scopes, the integrator's deep `elem.tangent`/`dof.tangent` leaves surface as *siblings* of `formTangent` in the rollup rather than nested under it (they nest correctly in `NewtonRaphson`). Accounting is complete either way; only the tree shape is affected. | [#650](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/650) |
