| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/pardiso/PARDISOGenLinSolver.cpp`, `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/pardiso/PARDISOGenLinSOE.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-75`: **stack-profiler brackets for PARDISO** — the phase split UmfPack got in ADR-40 (rank 8/10) but PARDISO landed without, so every ADR-75 profile showed one opaque `linearSolve` blob. Solver: `soe.symbolic` (phase 11), `soe.factor` (22), `soe.trisolve` (33) — **same names as `UmfpackGenLinSolver`**, so a cross-solver profile lines the phases up — plus PARDISO-only `soe.cgs` (phase 23): its own bracket because `-krylov`'s whole point is replacing a factor+trisolve pair, and a CGS give-up refactorizes INSIDE the phase-23 call (Intel's automatic fallback), so that cost bills to `soe.cgs`, not `soe.factor` (iparm[19]/-stats says how often). SOE: `dc.s.fill`/`dc.s.verify` setSize sub-brackets (the O(nnz×rowlen) sorted insertion vs the P1d contract sweep — MumpsParallelSOE's `dc.s.*` convention) and **DEEP-gated `soe.addA`** — per-element scatter attribution (the kernel-vs-scatter split ADR-75b L3-0 needed, previously only obtainable by differencing harness runs); deep-gated because a coarse bracket would tax the assembly loop of a performance feature. Coarse-off cost = one predicted branch (ScopedTimerOpt). Verified: `tests/test_pardiso_solver.py::test_profiler_brackets_present` — one deep `-krylov` run touches all 7 brackets (asserted as HDF5 group names on raw bytes, no h5py), with a coarse-run negative control (`soe.addA`/`soe.cgs` absent) proving both the deep gating and that the byte-scan is not vacuous; full 8-test parity battery re-passed on the instrumented binary. | [#667](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/667) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp`, `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-75 P1i`: **fix the profiler run-header size normalizers** — one `ops_profiler_fillModelMeta(meta, domain)` call added at each of the **four** `buildMeta()` sites (`profiler report` + `profiler checkpoint`, which exist **twice**: once in the Python ladder and once in the completely separate Tcl one — the banked "wiring one does not wire the other" trap, which is exactly how this drifted). Fills `nElem`/`nNode` (previously a hard 0 that a `Profiler.cpp` comment *claimed* the command layer populated — it only ever filled `nDOF`) and, under `-D_PARDISO`, overrides `threads` with `mkl_get_max_threads()`. Both TUs carry `-D_PARDISO` while `OPS_Utility` does not, which is why the MKL branch lives in a header these two include rather than in the profiler core. Reporting-only; no analysis behaviour changes. | [#674](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/674) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesPatternCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: **`sp ... -subtractInit` was a NO-OP on the openseespy path.** `OPS_SP()` handled the flag with `retZeroInitValue = true` — already the initialiser at `:1123` — so the branch did nothing and incremental SP was unreachable from Python, while **both** Tcl parsers set it to `false` correctly (`SRC/modelbuilder/tcl/TclModelBuilder.cpp:3808-3810`, `SRC/runtime/commands/modeling/constraint.cpp:392-394`). A Python-vs-Tcl behaviour split, not a missing feature. Consequence: a staged deck (gravity, then an imposed displacement) silently **yanked the node to the absolute value** instead of moving it incrementally from its staged state — wrong answer, no warning. Measured A/B on the same deck: `-subtractInit` gave **3.0 before / 5.5 after**, where Tcl already gave 5.5 on the unfixed binary. One-token change (`true` → `false`) plus the comment that records why. Only handlers reading `SP_Constraint::getInitialValue()` honour the flag (`TransformationDOF_Group:1068`, `PenaltySP_FE:125`, `LagrangeSP_FE:143`); under `constraints Plain` it is inert by design, fixed or not. Gated by `tests/test_sp_subtract_init.py` (verified to FAIL on the pre-fix binary). | [#675](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/675) |
| `SRC/element/Element.cpp` | `index == -1` self-heal calls the **virtual** `setRayleighDampingFactors`, so any element overriding it leaves `index == -1` and the next line dereferences `theMatrices[-1]` — access violation in `getRayleighDampingForces`, `getResistingForceIncInertia`, the 5 sensitivity getters and `getGeometricTangentStiff`. One-qualifier fix (`this->Element::`) at 11 sites. **Not fork-specific — upstream's own `Subdomain` overrides the virtual as a forwarder to `Domain::` and hits it too.** | [#676](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/676) |
| `SRC/analysis/handler/AutoConstraintHandler.cpp`, `SRC/analysis/handler/AutoConstraintHandler.h` | `// Ladruno (ADR-80 S2)`: **`constraints Auto` + a non-homogeneous `sp` was a SILENT WRONG ANSWER.** `AutoConstraintHandler::applyLoad()` ran the two `enforceSPs` loops but omitted the `updateElement()` loop that `TransformationConstraintHandler::enforceSPs` has at `:518-521`. Both handlers route SP-constrained nodes through a `TransformationDOF_Group`, so the DOF is genuinely eliminated (`setID(dof,-1)`) and there is no `K*du_prescribed` column — the **only** way the prescribed motion reaches the RHS is through the state of the elements attached to the driven node. Without the loop the first residual of every step is stale, `dU ~ 0`, and a dU-based convergence test declares victory at iteration 1 (`CTestNormDispIncr` has no minimum-iteration guard, `:160-175`); the step commits with **only the driven layer moved** and `analyze()` returns 0. Measured A/B on two equal trusses driven to 3.0 (ADR-80 gate G4, pre-fix binary): `Transformation` u_mid = **1.5 in 2 iterations**, `Auto` u_mid = **0.0 in 1 iteration**; identical signature on two stacked `stdBrick`s. **Test-dependence is the mechanism** and is why it hid: `NormDispIncr` *and* `EnergyIncr` both commit the wrong answer, while `NormUnbalance` sees the large stale residual, refuses to converge at iteration 1, and the next `LoadControl::update` → `updateDomain()` silently repairs it — the same model is right or wrong purely by choice of test. (The ADR-80 findings note predicted `CTestNormDispIncr` only; `EnergyIncr` is an additional casualty found at G4.) Fix mirrors the Transformation handler: a `theFEs` member holding the FE_Elements that touch an SP-constrained node (membership computed in `handle()` from the existing `sp_map`, O(1) per node), the `updateElement()` loop in `applyLoad()`, and a `clearAll()` reset. Answer-changing **by design** for the broken case; homogeneous-only (`fix`-only) models are numerically unaffected — `enforceSPs` rewrites an unchanged value, so the added update is idempotent. Gated by `tests/test_auto_handler_sp_update.py` (verified to FAIL on the pre-fix binary). **Fork-only (decided 2026-08-04) — deliberately NOT offered upstream**; it would apply cleanly there, but upstreaming a behaviour-changing handler fix is its own workstream and was not opened. | [#697](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/697) |
| `SRC/material/nD/soil/PressureDependMultiYield.cpp`, `SRC/material/nD/soil/PressureDependMultiYield.h` | `// Ladruno`: **PDMY01 had no plastic strain at all** — it stores nested yield surfaces, the PPZ/dilation state and the current stress, but nothing you could contour to see where the soil actually yielded, so a failure mechanism (e.g. the ADR-79 punching-vs-general-shear question) could only be inferred from displacements. Adds one accumulator, `accumPlasticStrain()`, called once per **sub-step** inside `getStress()`, which splits that sub-step's strain increment with the SAME hypoelastic moduli `setTrialStress()` just used: `d_eps^p = d_eps − dev(d_sigma)/(2G) − (d_p/(3B))·I`, with `G`/`B` cached into `elasticShearMod`/`elasticBulkMod` at the bottom of `setTrialStress()` (they carry the pressure-dependent `modulusFactor` and the `Hv` volumetric-plasticity modification, so the inversion is exact by construction). Anything the elastic predictor did not produce is plastic: surface return in `stressCorrection()`, dilatancy through the plastic potential, and the `p <= 0` tension cut-off. Three new recordable channels: `plasticStrain` (tensor, engineering shear, 3-component in 2D matching the existing `strain` view), `equivalentPlasticStrain`/`plasticStrainEq`/`ebarP` (`int sqrt(2/3 de^p_dev:de^p_dev)` — the von Mises convention, deliberately the same as `LadrunoJ2`'s `ebarP`), and `volumetricPlasticStrain`/`plasticStrainVol` (signed trace — the dilatancy signal, which is the one a soil modeller reads). Trial accumulators restart from committed at the top of `getStress()` (that routine re-integrates the whole step from the committed state on every call) and are committed alongside the surfaces. **Passive on the response path** — the accumulators are write-only new members, nothing feeds back into stress or tangent; existing PDMY tests re-pass unchanged. **Wire format changed:** `sendSelf`/`recvSelf` grow by 7 doubles. **Corrected in a follow-up (see the row below): the 7 are APPENDED past the surfaces at `70+n*8 .. 76+n*8`, NOT inserted at 70.** Gated by `tests/test_pdmy01_plastic_strain.py` (8 cases): stage-0 accrues exactly zero; `ebarP` monotone; **an elastic unload window after yielding accrues 1e-19 against a 1e-6 scale** (the sharp gate — it proves the hypoelastic inversion is exact, moduli and all); a full numpy reconstruction from the recorded (stress, strain) history matching to **1e-9 relative** on every component; plastic compaction on a sub-PT-line path; and the 2D reduced view. Multi-sub-step integration cross-checked separately (a coarse `nSub=2` run lands on the same step-refinement curve as the fine `nSub=1` runs — 3.73e-7 vs 3.51e-7 — not the ~2x a double-count would give). **Note:** PDMY02/03 are separate classes and do NOT get this. The oracle's exactness depends on two upstream traps recorded in [[LEDGER_quirks]] (cohesion really defaults to `0.3` though the usage string says `.5`; `residualPress` is stored positive while `refPressure` is negative). | [#708](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/708) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp`, `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.h`, `SRC/interpreter/PythonWrapper.cpp`, `SRC/interpreter/TclWrapper.cpp`, `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp`, `SRC/analysis/integrator/CMakeLists.txt`, `SRC/classTags.h` | `// Ladruno (ADR-80 S1)`: register the `LadrunoLoadControl` static integrator and its `ladrunoLoadControl` runtime command. `OpenSeesCommands.cpp` — `#include <LadrunoLoadControl.h>`, a `LadrunoLoadControl` branch in the `integrator` type ladder, and `OPS_LadrunoLoadControlCmd()` (subcommands `setDeltaLambda\|deltaLambda\|extrapolate\|armed`, guarded on `getClassTag() != INTEGRATOR_TAGS_LadrunoLoadControl`) — the command exists because `integrator LadrunoLoadControl ...` CONSTRUCTS A NEW OBJECT, so a caller-driven adaptive ladder that re-issues it every step destroys the predictor state and the predictor never fires; `.h` — the two externs; `{Python,Tcl}Wrapper.cpp` — the `ladrunoLoadControl` command shims; `commands.cpp` — the legacy `specifyIntegrator()` branch; `CMakeLists.txt` — the new source. Element/integrator body itself is fork-only (`SRC/analysis/integrator/LadrunoLoadControl.{h,cpp}`, derives from `StaticIntegrator` per the LadrunoArcLength precedent, so there is no LoadControl subclassing footprint). **Footprint note (corrected in #706):** #700 records 10,543 changed lines across `OpenSeesCommands.{cpp,h}`, but `diff --strip-trailing-cr` puts the REAL change at **63 lines**. The rest is a plain CRLF→LF flip of the whole file — parent blob 5,240 LF / 5,240 CR, child 5,303 LF / **0** CR. This was NOT negligence: `.gitattributes` marked both files `-text`, so git stored whatever bytes the editor emitted and neither ending was canonical. Diff those two files with `--strip-trailing-cr` before auditing this fork's vanilla footprint, or #700 reads as a 10k-line core rewrite that it is not. Root cause FIXED in [#706](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/706) — explicit `text` override on the registration surface, zero-diff because all eight files were already LF. See `Ladruno_internal/WORKFLOW_GOTCHAS.md` §6a. | [#700](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/700), ledger rows backfilled [#703](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/703), churn root cause fixed [#706](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/706) |
| `.gitattributes` | `# --- Ladruno: re-enable EOL normalization on the shared registration surface ---`: an override block appended at the END of the file (last matching line wins) turning `text` back ON for the eight files every fork feature touches to register a class — `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.{cpp,h}`, `SRC/interpreter/{Python,Tcl}Wrapper.cpp`, `SRC/interpreter/OpenSees{Element,Pattern}Commands.cpp`, `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp`, `SRC/classTags.h`. WHY: the inherited upstream `.gitattributes` is 5,345 lines of which **5,323 are per-file `-text`** (a CVS/SVN→git conversion artifact); `-text` means git stores bytes VERBATIM and normalizes nothing, so those files have no canonical line ending and any contributor whose editor differs rewrites the whole file — how #700 recorded 10,543 changed lines for 63 real ones. All eight targets were already `i/lf` in the blob, so this is a **ZERO-DIFF** change (`git add --renormalize` touches no source byte); verified A/B by CRLF-ifying a covered file (staged diff **empty**) vs the still-`-text` `OpenSeesCommandsTcl.cpp` (**188/188** whole-file churn). Deliberately NOT a repo-wide renormalize: ~447 blobs are still CRLF and rewriting them would be permanent whole-file divergence from upstream. | [#706](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/706) |
| `SRC/material/nD/soil/PressureDependMultiYield.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: **follow-up to the PDMY01 plastic-strain row above — make the `sendSelf`/`recvSelf` growth strictly ADDITIVE.** The first cut inserted the 7 plastic-strain doubles at slots 70..76 and pushed every yield surface from its upstream offset `70 + i*8` to `77 + i*8`. That was *self-consistent* — send and recv agreed, the round-trip passed, the numbers were right — but it **rewrote the vanilla layout** rather than extending it, which is precisely what §6 of [[../Ladruno_internal/WORKFLOW_GOTCHAS]] forbids for vanilla files ("strictly additive, never alter existing behavior") and which would make the eventual upstream diff gratuitously large. Now the surfaces stay on `70 + i*8` and the accumulators are appended at `pOff = 70 + numOfSurfaces*8`, so **the upstream layout is a strict prefix of ours**. No behaviour change and no numeric change — same 7 doubles, same values, different slots. **This does NOT restore cross-version database compatibility** and no honest fix can: `recvSelf` allocates `Vector data(...)` before the read, so an old file's shorter record still mismatches, and the `ID` header has no version field to negotiate with (adding one breaks the same way). A database written by a pre-#704 binary does not restore into a post-#704 one, full stop. What this buys is upstreamability and a minimal diff, not compatibility. **New gate:** `test_sendself_recvself_round_trip` — the format had **no coverage at all** before, which is what let the layout choice through unexamined. It save/restores through a `database File`, asserts every channel bit-identical, asserts the derived mobilised ratio (which is computed from `committedSurfaces[NYS].size()` and is therefore a direct probe of the surface block's offset), and then **keeps driving the model 10 steps** and compares against an uninterrupted reference. **Mutation-verified:** shifting only the `recvSelf` surface offset 70→71 sends the mobilised ratio to `inf` and the test fails — so the gate has teeth. Note the test pins `algorithm Newton`: `restore` resets the algorithm, Krylov's accumulator restarts with it, and PDMY commits `currentStress = trialStress` from the second-to-last Newton iterate, so a restarted Krylov run diverges ~6e-4 relative *with every byte of state correctly restored*; memoryless Newton reproduces to ~4e-14, which is what lets the assertion carry a real tolerance. | [#708](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/708) |
| `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/ManzariDafalias.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: **TIMs report safety pack, three traps.** (1, item 7) The two full constructors used by the Tcl/Python `nDMaterial ManzariDafalias` command never set the STATIC, class-wide `mElastFlag`, so it kept its file-scope default of `1` (ELASTOPLASTIC) — even though the parallel/classTag constructor already sets it to `0` and the community staged-gravity idiom (elastic gravity, then `updateMaterialStage -material N -stage 1`) assumes the material starts elastic. Without an explicit stage-0 call, "elastic" gravity ran the full plastic model from zero stress (a singular regime) and stalled Newton with **no diagnostic at all**. Fixed by adding `mElastFlag = 0;` to both full constructors, matching the parallel ctor. (2, item 8) `Elastic2Plastic()`'s "Outside Bounding!" `opserr` warning was commented out upstream, so a stage-switch stress ratio above the bounding surface silently bumps `M_c` by 10% with no trace; restored as a warning reporting the material tag, old `M_c`, and new `M_c` (numerics unchanged — upstream's behaviour is kept, only the silence is fixed). (3, item 3/4) Integration schemes 3 (`RungeKutta4`, whose adaptive branch is dead code, `if (false)`) and 5 (`ForwardEuler`) take one uncorrected substep with no error estimate and no yield-drift correction — a reported triaxial characterisation came out 31-46% too strong on scheme 3 before the reporters caught it. Added a once-per-process `opserr` warning (static-bool latch) in both full constructors when `mScheme == 3 \|\| mScheme == 5`, pointing at scheme 1 (ModifiedEuler) or 45 (RK45 Sloan) instead. See [[LEDGER_quirks]] for the static-flag caveat and the ssp+ManzariDafalias interaction. Gated by `tests/test_manzari_safety_pack.py`. | [#714](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/714) |
| `SRC/element/tetrahedron/TenNodeTetrahedron.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: **TIMs report item 8, TenNodeTetrahedron half.** `update()` called `materialPointers[i]->setTrialStrain(strain)` in the Gauss loop, discarded the return code (`success = ...` overwritten each iteration, never read), and unconditionally `return 0;` — so the element could never report a constitutive failure to the analysis, unlike its fork sibling `BezierTet10::update()`, which already accumulates the sum (`ret += theMaterial[gp]->setTrialStrain(...)`). Changed to `int success = 0;` at declaration and `success += ...` in the loop, `return success;` at the end — a pure accumulate-and-propagate fix, no numerics touched. **No dedicated regression test added** (a constitutive-failure-propagation probe on TenNodeTetrahedron was judged out of scope for this PR); `tests/test_manzari_safety_pack.py` and `tests/test_beziertet10_unsym_tangent.py`'s `TenNodeTetrahedron` control both continue to pass on the changed binary as an indirect no-regression check. | [#714](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/714) |
| `SRC/interpreter/PythonModule.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: **harden module init/shutdown against same-DLL re-import** (the LEDGER_quirks "re-import ... crashes Python AT SHUTDOWN" entry). `moduledef.m_size > 0` makes CPython RE-RUN `PyInit_opensees` when the module is re-imported after a `sys.modules.pop` of the same pyd; the init unconditionally called `Py_AtExit(cleanupFunc)`, so cleanup registered twice, and `cleanupFunc` deref'd the static `PythonModule* module` BEFORE its null check and never nulled it after `delete` — the second atexit call at `Py_FinalizeEx` wiped a dangling pointer (0xC0000005/0xC0000409, heap-state dependent). Two-part fix: `Py_AtExit` behind a `static bool` (register exactly once per TU), and a null-safe idempotent `cleanupFunc` (`if (module) { wipe; delete; module = 0; }`). One TU per Python target (`PythonMPIModule.cpp` re-includes this file), so `openseesmp` inherits the fix. Gated by the 3-line repro: `python3.12 -c "import sys, opensees; sys.modules.pop('opensees'); import opensees"` — exit −1073741819 pre-fix, 0 post-fix. | [#712](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/712) |
| `SRC/element/tetrahedron/TenNodeTetrahedron.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: **`setResponse` accepted ONLY the plural token spellings.** The two continuum branches were bare `strcmp(argv[0], "stresses")` / `"strains"`, so `-E stress` (singular) returned a null `Response` and the element was dropped from the recorder — silently, because the orchestrator had no diagnostic for a null response (fixed in the same PR). Neighbouring elements (`LadrunoBrick`, `FourNodeQuad`) take both spellings, so a mixed model recorded some element classes and not others from the SAME request. Both sites now route through `LadrunoResp::is(argv[0], "stress"/"strain")` (`SRC/element/LadrunoResponseTokens.h`, the canonical alias table introduced in cb292918b), which matches the singular AND the plural. **Alias only — nothing about the emitted response changes**: same `responseID` (3/4), same `ResponseType` labels, same `Vector(6*4)` width, so COLUMN_MAP layouts and MPCO/STKO readers are untouched. Gated by `tests/test_recorder_silent_drop.py::test_tet10_accepts_the_singular_token` (asserts 24 values for the singular spelling AND payload-identity with the plural); `tests/test_tet10_response_size.py` re-passes unchanged. | [#715](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/715) |
| `SRC/element/tetrahedron/FourNodeTetrahedron.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: same singular-token defect and same fix as the `TenNodeTetrahedron` row above — `strcmp(argv[0], "stresses")`/`"strains"` → `LadrunoResp::is(argv[0], "stress")`/`"strain"`, plus the `LadrunoResponseTokens.h` include. | `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/pardiso/PARDISOGenLinSOE.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno TIMs item 2` (ADR-75 P1d follow-up): **the `addA` half-storage asymmetry guard sampled STARTUP ONLY.** P1d's guard catches an unsymmetric element matrix whose lower half `-matrixType 1|2` is about to discard, but it was budgeted to the first `ASYM_CHECK_BUDGET`=3 tangent assemblies of the pattern (armed in `setSize`, spent one unit per pass in `zeroA`) — so the exact reported case, a material that is symmetric while elastic and only turns unsymmetric after first yield (non-associated flow, `rho_bar != rho`; ManzariDafalias measured at 4-6%), spent the whole budget elastic and was never checked again. Contact closing mid-run is the same hole. The in-source comment stated it as a flat LIMITATION rather than fixing it. **Fix:** a new `asymPass` member counts tangent assemblies of the current pattern (incremented in `zeroA`, re-anchored in `setSize`) and RE-ARMS the budget every `ASYM_RESAMPLE_PERIOD`=64 assemblies while `asymWarned` is unlatched — so the guard samples startup AND periodically, and late-onset asymmetry is reported within ≤64 assemblies of appearing. The once-per-SOE `asymWarned` latch is unchanged (it stops the re-arming for good), and the warning now names the assembly it fired on so the user can correlate it with the load step where the material yielded. **ADR-75's performance contract holds by arithmetic:** steady-state duty cycle is `ASYM_CHECK_BUDGET/ASYM_RESAMPLE_PERIOD` = 3/64 ≈ 4.7% of the mirror-compare cost, and that compare is the cheap half of `addA` (idSize²/2 in-cache subtractions of values already fetched, against an idSize² binary-searched scatter, no allocation, no extra traversal); 96.9% of assemblies pay one modulo in `zeroA` — once per assembly, not per element. Still a sampler, not a proof: an asymmetry that appears and vanishes entirely inside one 64-assembly sleep window is missable, and `-matrixType 2` still requires the author to know their tangent is symmetric. Gated by `tests/test_pardiso_asym_rearm.py`, which asserts not just that the warning fires but that the assembly number it reports is ≥64 — i.e. that it came from a RE-ARM, which the pre-change guard could not produce. | [#713](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/713) |

| `SRC/element/zeroLength/ZeroLengthND.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (audit following #709): **`getTangentStiff()`/`getInitialStiff()` assembled only the lower triangle of `A^T*kb*A` and mirrored it** (`for (j < i+1)` then `stiff(j,i)=stiff(i,j)`), which silently symmetrizes an unsymmetric `theNDMaterial->getTangent()`/`getInitialTangent()` — exactly the BezierTri6/BezierTet10 defect #709 fixed, found here on a second element family. Non-associated plasticity on a `zeroLengthND` element therefore corrupted Newton with no diagnostic. Fixed both functions to assemble the FULL `(i,j)` range and dropped the mirror loop; the scalar `the1DMaterial` term (inherently symmetric, a scalar times an outer product) is unaffected numerically. **Audit line-number drift note:** the audit's suggested repro material, `DruckerPrager3D` (order 6), cannot actually drive this element — it hits an UNRELATED pre-existing latent defect in `ZeroLengthND::setTransformation` (the fixed 3x3 `transformation` member is indexed by material order, so `order > 3` reads out of bounds); the regression test uses `DruckerPragerPlaneStrain` (order 3, same `rho_bar != rho` non-associated flow) instead, which exercises the identical assembly loop this PR changed without tripping the unrelated OOB. Gated by `tests/test_upstream_symmetrize_fixes.py::test_zerolengthnd_unsym_tangent`. | [#720](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/720) |
| `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/DruckerPragerPlaneStrain.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (audit following #709): **`getInitialTangent()` was a verbatim copy of `getTangent()`**, returning the condensed elastoplastic `mCep` (current, possibly unsymmetric under non-associated flow) instead of the elastic `mCe` — so the "initial" tangent drifted with plastic loading and was call-order dependent, unlike `DruckerPrager3D::getInitialTangent()` which correctly returns `mCe`. Fixed by condensing `mCe` the same way `getTangent()` condenses `mCep` (the same plane-strain component selection: `(0,0),(0,1),(0,3),(1,0),(1,1),(1,3),(3,0),(3,1),(3,3)`); `mCe` is set once in the base `DruckerPrager` constructor/`elastic_integrator()` and never mutated by plastic loading, so the result is now call-order independent. Gated by `tests/test_upstream_symmetrize_fixes.py::test_druckerpragerplanestrain_initial_tangent_stable`. | [#720](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/720) |
| `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/ContactMaterial2D.{h,cpp}`, `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/ContactMaterial3D.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno` (audit following #709): **`getInitialTangent()` returned the SAME `tangent_matrix` buffer `getTangent()` overwrites in place**, so the "initial" tangent depended on call order (whatever sliding/sticking state the last `getTangent()` call left behind) and was the ZERO matrix — a singular initial stiffness — on a freshly constructed material before any `getTangent()` call. Added a dedicated `initialTangent` member (sized in every constructor) populated independently with the elastic (sticking, within-tensile-strength-bound) tangent: 2D = `{(0,2)=1,(1,1)=stiffness,(2,0)=1}` (the "sticking coefficients" branch of `getTangent()`, genuinely call-order independent); 3D = `{(0,3)=1,(1..2,1..2)=stiffness*g,(3,0)=1}` using the metric tensor `g` set by the enclosing contact element's `setMetricTensor()` — call-order independent once geometrically configured, and correctly zero (not a bug) if `setMetricTensor()` was never called. **Unreachable from openseespy** (see `test_contactmaterial_initial_tangent_unreachable_from_python`, skipped with reason): every upstream element owning one of these materials (`SimpleContact2D/3D`, `BeamContact2D/3D/2Dp/3Dp`) overrides `getInitialStiff()` as a bare `return getTangentStiff();` and never calls the material's own `getInitialTangent()`; `ZeroLengthND` cannot substitute either since `ContactMaterial{2,3}D::getCopy(const char*)` only matches its own class-name string, not `"PlaneStrain2D"`/`"ThreeDimensional"`. Verified by code inspection only. | [#720](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/720) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/profileSPD/ProfileSPDLinSOE.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno` (audit following #709 / modelled on the PARDISO #713 guard): **`ProfileSPDLinSOE::addA` unconditionally keeps only `row <= col` ("we only add upper and inside profile") and silently discards the lower triangle — with no diagnostic at all** — and `ProfileSPD` is the DEFAULT solver constructed by both interpreters (`OpenSeesCommands.cpp` ~326/~696/~870) when no `system` command is given. A non-associated-plasticity or contact tangent therefore gets symmetrized by default with no warning. Added the same budgeted, once-per-SOE `asymWarned`/`asymBudget`/`asymPass` detector as `PARDISOGenLinSOE` (`ASYM_CHECK_BUDGET`=3, `ASYM_RESAMPLE_PERIOD`=64; armed in `setSize`, spent/re-armed in `zeroA`, checked in `addA` against the local element matrix `m(ii,jj)` vs `m(jj,ii)`), so the check samples startup AND periodically (catching late-onset asymmetry after first yield) while costing ~4.7% duty cycle of an already-cheap in-cache compare — negligible against the default solver's steady state. Message names `ProfileSPD` and recommends `FullGeneral`/`UmfPack`/`SparseGEN`/`Pardiso`. Gated by `tests/test_upstream_symmetrize_fixes.py::test_profilespd_asym_warning_once` / `::test_profilespd_asym_warning_absent_when_elastic`. | [#720](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/720) |
| `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp` | `// Ladruno` build-stamp query: classic-Tcl `ladrunoBuild` command (the `version` pattern — `Tcl_SetResult` of `OPENSEES_VERSION`) so `OpenSees.exe` answers without banner scraping. | [#718](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/718) |
| `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp`, `SRC/tcl/commands.h` | `// Ladruno` (#729, the ADR-78 P0.5 / #726 gap applied to the SOLVER-STATE queries): register `ladrunoArcLength` (ADR-20 Layer-B / ADR-31 rung 4) and `ladrunoDR` (ADR-31 rung 5) in the classic Tcl engine. They existed only in `TclWrapper.cpp` / `PythonWrapper.cpp`, so `OpenSees.exe` — and `OpenSeesMP`, which drives parallelism through this file — answered `invalid command name "ladrunoDR"` even though `integrator LadrunoDynamicRelaxation` itself parsed fine: a `.tcl` deck could START a dynamic relaxation but never read the settling gate that says when it is done. Adds `#include <LadrunoSolverQuery.h>`, two `Tcl_CreateCommand` registrations, two `OPS_ResetInputNoBuilder`-shaped bridges, and the two `commands.h` declarations (registrations sit ~5700 lines above the definitions). **NOT the #726 shape, for two measured reasons:** (a) the bridges pass `theStaticIntegrator` / `theTransientIntegrator` into the shared dispatch instead of calling the no-arg `OPS_Ladruno*Cmd()` forms — those read the `cmds` singleton only the DL engine constructs, so from classic Tcl they hit `cmds == 0`, return **0 (SUCCESS)** and write nothing, i.e. a command that exists and silently answers an empty string; (b) the dispatch could not be externed out of `OpenSeesCommands.cpp` at all — see the `LEDGER_quirks` row on link-line-vs-linked-in. Gated by `tests/test_ladruno_solver_queries_tcl.py` + `tests/tcl/ladruno_solver_queries.tcl`, which fail on an EMPTY result and not merely on a Tcl error. | [#729](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/729) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (#729): `OPS_LadrunoArcLengthCmd()` / `OPS_LadrunoDRCmd()` reduced to three-line wrappers — `if (cmds == 0) return 0; return Ladruno::{arcLength,dr}Command(cmds->get{Static,Transient}Integrator());` — after their ~130 lines of subcommand dispatch moved verbatim to the new header-only `SRC/analysis/integrator/LadrunoSolverQuery.h` so the classic Tcl engine can share ONE copy. `+#include <LadrunoSolverQuery.h>`. Behaviour-preserving for this engine: the DL lane's Zone-A DR (13) + ArcLength (11) batteries pass unchanged. | [#729](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/729) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (#728, extends the #243 block): third `ladrunoDR` subcommand `stabilityMargin` → `LadrunoDynamicRelaxation::getStabilityMargin()`, returning `(ω_max·dt/2)²` for the fictitious mass actually in use, measured against the tangent at the last `M*` rebuild (`≤1` stable, `== massSafety²` on an unchanged tangent, `>1` = marching at/over the explicit boundary where DR can relax to a SILENTLY wrong state, `−1` = no gershgorin bound). Also widens the two usage/`unknown subcommand` strings. Additive — no existing subcommand changed. | [#728](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/728) |
| `SRC/analysis/handler/AutoConstraintHandler.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (ADR-78 follow-up): **`constraints Auto` sized its penalty from ONE rank's elements under MPI, since the handler was contributed.** The four `MPI_Allreduce` calls that make `KAVG` global sat in an `#if defined(_PARALLEL_PROCESSING) || defined(_PARALLEL_INTERPRETERS)` block — and this file compiles into the `OPS_Analysis` OBJECT library, built ONCE with no parallel define and folded into every target, so the block compiled to nothing in **every** binary including `OpenSeesMP`. `PVAL = 10^(round(log10(KAVG))+oom)` is the value `getPenaltyValue()` falls back to for a constrained node with no locally attached elements — precisely a partition-straddling interface node — so ranks sharing a constraint sized it from disjoint stiffness samples with nothing printed. Reduction moved verbatim (same four collectives, same order, same `OPS_PARTITIONED` quick-return, same warning strings) to `LadrunoAutoPenaltyReduce.cpp`, which IS compiled per target. **This TU is now left with NO `#ifdef _PARALLEL_*` and an unconditional call** — that, not the guard's contents, is what keeps the trap from silently reopening. Scope stated honestly: restores the GLOBAL statistic only; per-node penalties stay rank-local (element-loop accumulation, would need element ghosting) and are far less severe since the distributed SOE sums both ranks' contributions. Serial builds unaffected — the call is a no-op returning 1. Gated by `Ladruno_files/testbed/auto_penalty_mpi/`. | [#733](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/733) |

The block comment's standing NB that `AutoConstraintHandler.cpp` had the same latent problem is replaced by the per-file list explaining why each TU is here. | [#733](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/733) |

| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesOutputCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (ADR-78 removal lane): `OPS_LadrunoContactSurface()` now REJECTS a node tag that does not exist at DECLARATION time. This is the load-bearing half of making `recorder Collapse` + contact legal: before it, a deck typo and a node the ANALYSIS removed mid-run were indistinguishable (both first surfaced inside `handle()`, since `LadrunoContactSurface` never saw the `Domain` and `addSurface()` did no existence check), which is why ADR-78 P1 had to abort on both and a collapse run died mid-analysis. Pinning the tags at declaration means later absence can only be a runtime removal, which `handle()` prunes. Strictly better on its own terms too: a typo now fails at the deck line that contains it, naming the tag. Placed in the parser rather than in `LadrunoContactDomain::addSurface()` (where the other choke-point guards live) because `addSurface()` has no `Domain` handle; this is the single parser for BOTH engines since ADR-78 P0.5 registered the Tcl verbs against it, so one check still covers Py + Tcl. | [#737](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/737) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesOutputCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (ADR-78 P4 finding 1 — closes the defect the row above opened): the three contact DECLARATION verbs each become a two-line wrapper (`OPS_LadrunoContactSurface` / `OPS_LadrunoContact` / `OPS_LadrunoContactPlane`) over their unchanged bodies (now `static ladrunoContact*Impl()`), routing any `<0` result through `ladrunoContactFatal()`. The declaration-time refusal above is a rank-local parser `return -1` with no MPI awareness: the refusing rank aborts its script, every peer blocks in the next collective, and the job HANGS — measured on the preserved P0 mutation deck `contact_parallel/mp_noghost.tcl`, which P1 measured at a 1.1 s teardown and which hung >30 s / 45 s (once a nondeterministic 17 s hydra reap) after #737 moved the check out of `handle()`. Wrapping the RESULT rather than editing the ~75 individual `return -1` sites is deliberate: it also covers the refusals that propagate out of `addSurface`/`addContact`/`addMortarContact`/`addRigidPlane` (unknown surface tag, duplicate tag — the other partition-dependent class), and a check added later inherits the teardown instead of silently reopening this defect. Serial and `mpiexec -n 1` byte-identical (INV-5): `ladrunoContactFatal()` returns −1 untouched at np ≤ 1. The QUERY verbs are deliberately NOT wrapped — their −1 is legitimately rank-local (`ladrunoMortarTieResidual` is 0 on the non-owner by design). | [#742](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/742) |
| `SRC/analysis/handler/LadrunoContactHandler.cpp` | `// Ladruno` (ADR-78 removal lane): `handle()` calls `LadrunoContactDomain::pruneRemovedNodes()` before anything reads a surface, and the three interaction loops skip a `retired` interaction. `handle()` re-runs on every `domainChanged()`, so this is what reconciles a contact declared once at setup with a domain the analysis keeps shrinking. The `-kn auto` / `-soft` / `kn <= 0` aborts are deliberately untouched — apeGmsh ADR 0092 INV-1's owner-rank proxy is safe only because `-kn auto` failure is fatal, and `tests/test_contact_runtime_removal.py::test_kn_auto_failure_is_still_fatal` guards that guard. | [#737](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/737) |

| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/fullGEN/FullGenLinSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable** (pure upstream defect, no fork concepts -- another candidate for the [[upstream_pr_campaign]] small-correctness wave). `// Ladruno`: `getA()`, `getX()` and `getB()` called **`exit(-1)`** when their wrapper was null -- whole-process death, exit code -1/255, and from Python a SILENT one (`exit()` unwinds nothing, so no traceback and no exception; `opserr` is redirected under the pyd; `faulthandler` stays quiet because it is a clean exit, not a signal). This is the SECOND door onto the same family as the zero-free-equation rows above, and the one a USER can walk through from the interpreter: the wrappers are allocated in `setSize()`, which `StaticAnalysis::domainChanged()` reaches only AFTER `ConstraintHandler::handle()` returns `>= 0`, so **any** route that leaves the SOE unsized turned `printA` -- the standard tangent-extraction diagnostic -- into a dead interpreter. Now each reports itself and returns an EMPTY result: `getA()` returns 0 (exactly what `LinearSOE::getA()` returns by default, so every caller already branches on it -- `OPS_printA` and `LinearSOE::saveSparseA` both do), `getX()`/`getB()` return a shared size-0 `Vector` from a function-local static (`OPS_printB` already has a `size == 0` branch). Callers inside the analysis flow are untouched -- they only run post-`domainChanged()`, where the wrappers exist. Also fixes a copy-paste bug found in passing: `getA()`'s FATAL text read `"FullGenLinSOE::getB - vectB == 0"` while testing `matA`. **Why it presented as a contact bug:** under the upstream handlers `handle()` essentially never fails, but the contact subsystem's entire ADR-78/ADR-85 abort discipline is BUILT on `handle()` returning -1 via `ladrunoContactFatal()` for a refused contact -- so a failed `domainChanged()` is a routine, designed outcome there, and the next thing a user reaches for to debug the refusal is `printA`. Gated by `tests/test_printa_unsized_soe.py` (zone_a, subprocess-isolated; measured pre-fix: 7 of 12 rows fail, the `UmfPack` control rows pass). See [[LEDGER_quirks]]. | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/fullGEN/FullGenLinSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable** (pure upstream defect, three tokens, no fork concepts). `// Ladruno`: `setSize()` dimensioned its `vectX`/`vectB`/`matA` wrappers by **`Bsize`** -- a grow-only high-water CAPACITY -- instead of `size`, the LIVE equation count. They diverge the moment an SOE SHRINKS (a second analysis with more DOFs constrained, staged construction, `remove element`, a contact set that retires), and the wrappers then described the OLD, larger system over the same storage. **Nothing crashed** -- `Bsize**2 == Asize` exactly, so the read stayed in bounds -- it returned a plausible WRONG ANSWER, which is this fork's booked worst case. MEASURED on a 12 -> 6 equation shrink: `systemSize()` correctly said 6 while `printA("-ret")` returned **144** values (the stale 12x12) and `printB("-ret")` **12** -- 108 stale entries handed back as the tangent, silently. `size` is right by construction: `addA` indexes `A + col*size` and `addB`/`setB` index over `[0,size)`, so the live system is exactly `size x size` from `A[0]`. **FullGen was the lone outlier** -- BandGen, BandSPD, ProfileSPD, SProfileSPD, Diagonal, SparseGenCol and SymSparse all already build their wrappers with `size` (surveyed). Only the wrapper EXTENT changes; allocation still tracks capacity, so no reallocation behaviour moves. Found while reviewing this file for the unsized-SOE work -- it is the OTHER way `printA` lies, and unlike the `exit(-1)` family it is reachable after a **successful** analyze. Gated by `tests/test_printa_unsized_soe.py::test_printa_after_shrink_reports_the_live_size` (uses `systemSize()` as the oracle -- asserting against `len(B)` would be circular, since `len(B)` is exactly what goes stale). | [#759](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/759) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesCommands.cpp` | **Upstreamable** (pure upstream usability defect). `// Ladruno`: `OPS_printA()`'s `-sparse <baseIndex>` argument is OPTIONAL -- the code guards it with `if (OPS_GetNumRemainingInputArgs() > 0)` -- but the READ was unconditional, so `printA -sparse -ret` (the natural way to ask for the sparse matrix BACK) consumed `-ret` as the base index, failed to parse it and returned -1 with `failed to read -sparse <baseIndex>`. The only working order was the non-obvious `printA -ret -sparse 0`, which makes the flags look positional when they are not. Now PEEKS with `OPS_GetString()` + `OPS_ResetCurrentInputArg(-1)` and consumes the token only when it is not an option flag. Classification rule REUSED from the contact parser's `ladrunoCountLeadingNumbers` (`OpenSeesOutputCommands.cpp`), including its caveat: a token is a FLAG only when `-` is followed by a LETTER, so a NEGATIVE NUMBER is still read as a number, and `OPS_GetString()` never returns null (it returns the literal `"Invalid String Input!"` for a non-string and at end of input) -- which is not `-`-prefixed and so counts as a number, the right answer for a numeric argument, while end-of-input cannot reach the branch because the remaining-args guard already excluded it. `-precision` has the same unconditional read but its argument is not optional in practice, so it is left alone. Found while verifying the SOE-shrink fix's sparse-export path. Gated by `tests/test_printa_unsized_soe.py::test_printa_sparse_flag_order_is_free` (all three orderings + the base-index shift; fails pre-fix on `sparse_then_ret`). | [#761](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/761) |
| `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp` | **Upstreamable.** `// Ladruno`: the CLASSIC-TCL twin of the `OPS_printA` `-sparse` fix in the row above. `SRC/tcl/commands.cpp` carries a SECOND, independent `printA` -- its own argv walk, and the one `OpenSees.exe`/`OpenSeesSP`/`OpenSeesMP` actually run -- with the identical defect, so fixing the openseespy path alone would have left the engine most `.tcl` decks use still broken and every Python gate still green. **That drift is a known failure mode on this fork, not a hypothetical**: see the [#729](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/729) row (`ladrunoArcLength`/`ladrunoDR` registered only in the Python wrappers, so a `.tcl` deck could START a dynamic relaxation but never read its settling gate) and the ADR-75 P2h row (a Tcl `system Mumps` ladder that silently DROPPED `-BLR`/`-stats`). The Tcl version was also marginally worse than the Python one: `currentArg++` ran BEFORE the bounds test, so a bare trailing `printA -sparse` (index omitted, nothing after it) survived only by luck -- the cursor landed on `argc` and the loop ended. Now peeks `argv[currentArg+1]` and consumes it only when it is not an option flag; Tcl hands every token over as a string, so the same `-`-followed-by-a-LETTER rule keeps a NEGATIVE base index readable as a number. Gated by `tests/test_printa_sparse_flag_order_tcl.py` + `tests/tcl/printa_sparse_flag_order.tcl` (4 orderings incl. the bare trailing form; the deck `catch`es each call so a refusal is a reported FAIL rather than an aborted deck, and the wrapper asserts the check COUNT so a deck that dies early cannot pass vacuously). | [#761](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/761) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/diagonal/DiagonalSOE.cpp` | `// Ladruno`: the twin of the `FullGenLinSOE.cpp` `exit(-1)`-in-the-accessors row above, same three methods, same fix. `DiagonalSOE` matters specifically because it and `FullGenLinSOE` are the **only two classes in the whole `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE` tree that override `getA()` at all** (surveyed) -- so they were the only two `system` choices whose `printA` could kill the interpreter, and every other system was safe purely by inheriting `LinearSOE::getA()`'s `return 0`. `Diagonal` appears in the unsized-SOE rows of the new gate but NOT in its positive control, for the reason its sibling file already records: it solves only the tangent diagonal, so a Newton static with real free equations is Jacobi iteration and does not converge (measured `rc = -3`). **Second, unrelated defect fixed in the follow-up pass:** the SIZED constructor `DiagonalSOE(int N, DiagonalSolver&, bool)` guarded its whole allocation block on `if (size > 0)` -- testing the member the init list had just set to `0`, with `size = N` living INSIDE the block -- so the guard could never be true and **the sized overload allocated nothing at all**, returning an SOE indistinguishable from the default-constructed one. Now guards on the PARAMETER (`if (N > 0)`). This cannot change behaviour for any existing caller because there are none: all three call sites (`SRC/tcl/commands.cpp` x2, `DiagonalDirectSolver.cpp`) use the 2-argument form, and `setSize()` allocates later regardless -- which is the only reason the dead block was ever harmless. Swept the rest of `SRC/system_of_eqn/` for the same copy-paste shape; this is the only instance. | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/sparseSYM/SymSparseLinSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable.** `// Ladruno`: two separate defects on the same unsized-SOE route. (a) `zeroA()` dereferenced NULL -- `penv[size]` on a null `penv`, then `blkPtr->beg` on a null `first`. This one was a genuine **ACCESS VIOLATION (measured 0xC0000005)**, not the `exit(-1)` family, and it is why `system SparseSYM` was the ONE system where `printA` still hard-crashed after the `getA()` fixes: this class does not override `getA()` (it inherits `LinearSOE`'s safe `return 0`), so the crash was entirely in `formTangent() -> zeroA()`, upstream of any accessor. `memset(diag, 0, size*sizeof(double))` on the line before happens to be harmless at size 0, which is why the fault landed on the next statement. Guarded with an early return on `size == 0 || penv == 0 || first == 0` (setting `factored = false`, as the normal path does). (b) the DESTRUCTOR had the same class of bug, one line further along the same null-`first` path: `while (1) { if (blkPtr->next == blkPtr) { if (blkPtr != NULL) ...` dereferences `blkPtr->next` BEFORE the null check inside the loop -- a check-AFTER-use, so destroying a never-sized SOE was an access violation at TEARDOWN. Changed to `while (blkPtr != 0)`, which is behaviour-preserving for a sized SOE (the loop still exits on its own `blkPtr->next == blkPtr` sentinel). **This one is worth knowing about for its failure SHAPE:** it fires on interpreter exit, AFTER the script's last output has flushed, so it reads as a clean run that happens to exit nonzero -- and a probe that greps stdout for a success line instead of checking the exit code reports PASS. Mine did exactly that and hid it; the pytest gate asserts on `returncode` and caught it. (c) the `getX()`/`getB()` `exit(-1)` pair, same fix as the rows below. | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/bandGEN/BandGenLinSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable.** `// Ladruno`: the `getX()`/`getB()` `exit(-1)`-on-null-wrapper pair (the 16-class family named in the `FullGenLinSOE.cpp` row above). Same fix as there: warn naming the class/method and the real cause, return a shared size-0 `Vector` from a function-local static. This reaches `printB`, NOT `printA` -- these classes inherit `LinearSOE::getA()`'s `return 0`, so `printA` was already safe on them. Measured before the fix, `printB` on an unsized SOE: 6 of 10 `system` choices died. `system BandGeneral`. | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |

| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/mumps/MumpsSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable** (pure upstream defect, no fork concepts -- [[upstream_pr_campaign]] small-correctness wave). `// Ladruno`: the MPI half of the unsized-SOE `exit(-1)` family opened by the `FullGenLinSOE.cpp` row above -- same `getX()`/`getB()` pair, same fix (warn naming class/method + cause, return a shared size-0 `Vector` from a function-local static). Deferred from the serial pass because **no serial desktop build compiles it**; this row lands it against a real `Ladruno_scripts\build.bat OpenSeesMP OpenSeesPyMP`. **Why the MPI half was the more dangerous one:** these solvers are collective in every phase, so `exit(-1)` on whichever rank happened to touch the accessor leaves its peers blocked in their next MPI call -- the job does not fail, it **HANGS** to the launcher timeout. Same failure shape as the rank-local `return -1` fixed in #742, and the same lesson: a diagnostic must never take down one rank unilaterally. Covers `MumpsParallelSOE` for free (derives from `MumpsSOE`, overrides neither accessor). Reachable as `system Mumps` (gated on `_MUMPS`); gated by the new MP rows in `tests/test_printa_unsized_soe.py`. | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/diagonal/MPIDiagonalSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable.** `// Ladruno`: TWO defects. (a) the `getX()`/`getB()` `exit(-1)` pair, same fix and same MPI-hang rationale as the `MumpsSOE.cpp` row above; reachable as `system MPIDiagonal` (gated on `_PARALLEL_INTERPRETERS`, i.e. `OpenSeesMP` only -- in a serial build that verb silently falls back to plain `DiagonalSOE`, so a serial "pass" on this system would be a FALSE pass, which is why the new gate's MP rows run under `mpiexec`). (b) `getpartofA()` had the same `exit(-1)` on a null raw `A`, **and its message named the wrong method** -- `"FATAL MPIDiagonalSOE::getA - A == 0"` inside `getpartofA` -- stale copy-paste from a `getA()` this class does not override, so the one clue a user got pointed at the wrong place. Now warns and returns a ZEROED `At` rather than a size-0 `Vector`, because `At` is the caller's already-sized buffer passed by reference: handing it back untouched would return whatever uninitialized garbage the caller allocated. Note `getpartofA` currently has **no callers anywhere in the tree**, so (b) is latent-defect hygiene, not an observed crash; left in place rather than deleted because removing public API is a separate decision. | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/diagonal/DistributedDiagonalSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable.** `// Ladruno`: the `getX()`/`getB()` `exit(-1)` pair, same fix and same MPI-hang rationale as the `MumpsSOE.cpp` row above. Reachability is worth recording because it is NOT what the filename suggests: this file is compiled into `OPS_SysOfEqn`, i.e. into **every** target including the serial desktop build (`diagonal/CMakeLists.txt` is unconditional), but it is only ever *instantiated* under `_PARALLEL_PROCESSING` -- Tcl `system Diagonal` in `OpenSeesSP` -- plus `FEM_ObjectBrokerAllClasses`' `recvSelf` path. openseespy's `system Diagonal` has no `#ifdef` and always yields plain `DiagonalSOE`. So it is **compile-verified but not exercised** by the new gate: there is no Python door to it. | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/sparseGEN/DistributedSparseGenRowLinSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable.** `// Ladruno`: the `getX()`/`getB()` `exit(-1)` pair, same fix as the `MumpsSOE.cpp` row above. **This class is dead code** and the fix is pure hygiene: it is compiled into `OpenSeesMP`/`OpenSeesSP` (guarded by `if(MPI_FOUND)` in `sparseGEN/CMakeLists.txt`) and it owns `LinSOE_TAGS_DistributedSparseGenRowLinSOE = 21` in `classTags.h`, but **nothing in the tree ever constructs it** -- not the Tcl `soe_table`, not `OpenSeesCommands.cpp`, not even `FEM_ObjectBrokerAllClasses` (which does have a case for its `DistributedDiagonalSOE` sibling). Every reference is an `#include`. Compile-verified only; unreachable, therefore untestable. | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/petsc/PetscSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable**, but **NOT COMPILED and therefore UNVERIFIED.** `// Ladruno`: the `getX()`/`getB()` `exit(-1)` pair, fixed to match the verified serial twins so a future PETSc revival does not carry the defect back in. `add_subdirectory(petsc)` is commented out in `linearSOE/CMakeLists.txt` (as is `mumps`, whose sources the top-level `CMakeLists.txt` instead adds straight to the SP/MP targets), so this file compiles in NO target and this edit has never been through a compiler. Flagged as such in-source. `cerr` is retained over `opserr` because every other diagnostic in the file uses it and there is no `opserr` include. **Corrects the handoff note** that `petsc/badPetscSOE.cpp` "is not in any CMake target": it IS listed in `petsc/CMakeLists.txt`, alongside `PetscSOE.cpp` and a stray `main.cpp` -- that whole file is simply never processed. `badPetscSOE.cpp` carries the identical defect in a stale copy of the same class and is deliberately left alone. | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/mumps/MumpsParallelSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable.** `// Ladruno`: **the one member of this family that was MEASURED crashing, and the one the `exit(`-grep survey missed.** `MumpsParallelSOE` overrides `getB()` (it does not override `getX()`, which comes from the fixed `MumpsSOE`) and the override had **no null check of any kind** -- straight to `*myVectB` / `*vectB`, null from the constructor until `setSize()`. So under MP, `system Mumps` + `printB` on an unsized SOE was a raw null dereference, NOT the `exit(-1)` family: measured `mpiexec -n 2`, rank 1 died `0xC0000005` and rank 0 went down with it (`EXIT STATUS: -1`). It had no `FATAL` text to grep for, which is exactly why a survey built on `grep exit(` did not see it -- **found instead by sweeping every `getX`/`getB` definition in the tree for the presence of a null guard.** The same sweep found four identical siblings (rows below). Bisected with a two-step probe: `printA` alone survives on both ranks (`len=0`), `printB` alone kills both -- so `getA`/`zeroA`/`formTangent` were already clean here and `getB` was the whole crash. **The design point in the fix:** this method is COLLECTIVE (workers send then receive, the master gathers from each and replies), so a guard firing on some ranks and not others would leave the rest blocked forever -- worse than the crash. It cannot diverge, because the wrappers are allocated in `setSize()`, reached only through the collective `domainChanged()`: in the unsized state NO rank has them, so EVERY rank returns early and the collective is simply never entered. Gated by the MP rows of `tests/test_printa_unsized_soe.py` (this row is what those rows caught). | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |
| `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/bandGEN/DistributedBandGenLinSOE.cpp` | **Upstreamable.** `// Ladruno`: byte-identical unguarded collective `getB()` to the `MumpsParallelSOE.cpp` row above -- same `*myVectB` / `*vectB` null dereference on an unsized SOE, same guard, same no-deadlock argument. Compiled into **every** target (`bandGEN/CMakeLists.txt` is unconditional), but only instantiated under `_PARALLEL_PROCESSING` via Tcl `system BandGeneral`; openseespy's `system BandGeneral` has no `#ifdef` and always yields plain `BandGenLinSOE`. **Compile-verified, not exercised** -- there is no Python door, so the MP gate cannot reach it. Fixed because it is the same defect in the same family, found by the same sweep. | [#754](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/754) |

| `SRC/material/nD/ASDPlasticMaterial3D/ASDPlasticMaterial3D.h` | `// Ladruno (ADR-84 P0)`: (a) `Backward_Euler` gains a `special_return` hook block, called after the elastic early-exit and before the scalar Newton — `if constexpr (yf_has_special_return<YFType>::value)` gates it, so for every existing YF (whose trait defaults false) it compiles away entirely and the method is byte-identical. Only `MohrCoulombTensionCutoff_YF` opts in. (b) The constructor zeroed its Eigen stress/strain members (`Trial*`/`Commit*`) with `*= 0` on freshly-allocated (uninitialized) heap storage — `NaN*0 == NaN`, so heap garbage from a churned allocator (pytest) survived construction and silently poisoned every yf comparison. Fixed to `setZero()`. Benefits ALL ASDP materials, not just MCTC — found during P0 verification, see [[LEDGER_quirks]]. | [#741](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/741) |
| `SRC/material/nD/ASDPlasticMaterial3D/YieldFunctionBase.h` | `// Ladruno (ADR-84 P0)`: additive `yf_has_special_return` trait (defaults `false`) + `SPECIAL_RETURN` opt-in macro that a YF can invoke to declare it implements the hook body `special_return()` called from `ASDPlasticMaterial3D::Backward_Euler`. No existing YF opts in; purely additive. | [#741](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/741) |
| `SRC/material/nD/ASDPlasticMaterial3D/YieldFunctions/AllYieldFunctions.h` | `// Ladruno (ADR-84 P0)`: `+1 #include "MohrCoulombTensionCutoff_YF.h"`. | [#741](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/741) |

| `SRC/material/nD/ASDPlasticMaterial3D/gen_ASD_material_definitions_CPP.py` | `// Ladruno (ADR-84 P0)`: one explicit `COMBINED_MODELS` entry (mirroring the existing `STIFFSOIL_MODELS` pattern) — `LinearIsotropic3D_EL × MohrCoulombTensionCutoff_YF × MohrCoulombTensionCutoff_PF × BackStress<NullHardeningTensorFunction>`. Deliberately NOT a cross-product registration (e.g. `MohrCoulombTensionCutoff_YF × MohrCoulomb_PF` is intentionally absent — dilatant MC shear flow on the tension cap is mechanically wrong, and every extra pairing grows the `OPS_AllASDPlasticMaterial3Ds.cpp` monster TU). +1 instantiation, 45 → 46. | [#741](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/741) |

| `SRC/material/nD/ASDPlasticMaterial3D/ASDPlasticMaterial3D.h` | `// Ladruno (ADR-84 P2a)`: fixes the two UPSTREAM silent-accept defects in `Backward_Euler`, both behind a new opt-in integration option `strict_convergence` (int, default 0 ⇒ byte-identical to upstream). (a) **Exhaustion-accept** — the scalar-Newton loop fell out of `max_iter` with no convergence check and dropped through to `ComputeTangentStiffness(); return 0;`, committing `f > tol_yf` as success (the persistence mechanism behind the Cerro Lindo M3 finding, 20 GPs at `f/(2c·cosφ) = +0.0299`). Now tracked by a `be_converged` flag; when the flag is on and the loop exhausts with `|Phi| >= tol_yf`, an `opserr` line naming tag/`|Phi|`/tol is printed and the method returns -1. `Phi` is re-evaluated after the loop because the last Newton update is never checked inside it. (b) **f-decreasing elastic early-exit** — the second disjunct accepted a step as elastic whenever `f` merely decreased by more than `tol_yf`, even with `yf_val_end > 0`, perpetuating an inadmissible commit instead of correcting it; with the flag on it additionally requires `yf_val_end <= tol_yf` (elastic unloading, `f_end < 0`, still exits). Plumbing: `set_constitutive_integration_method` gains a defaulted 9th arg, `+1` per-tag static map `INT_OPT_strict_convergence` (+ its out-of-class definition). The ADR-84 P0 `special_return` hook is upstream of the Newton loop and unaffected in both modes. See [[LEDGER_quirks]] (two entries). | [#744](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/744) |
| `SRC/material/nD/ASDPlasticMaterial3D/OPS_AllASDPlasticMaterial3Ds.cpp` | `// Ladruno (ADR-84 P2a)`: parses `strict_convergence <int>` in the `Begin_Integration_Options` block (default 0, mirroring how `return_to_yield_surface` is plumbed), passes it as the new 9th argument of `set_constitutive_integration_method`, and documents it in `print_usage()`. Files also covering this PR's test: `tests/test_adr84_p2a_strict_convergence.py` (new, `zone_a`). | [#744](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/744) |
| `SRC/material/nD/ASDPlasticMaterial3D/YieldFunctionBase.h` | `// Ladruno (ADR-84 P3)`: the `SPECIAL_RETURN` macro gains a trailing `int& return_quality` out-param, and two `SR_QUALITY_*` codes are defined next to it. `stiffness_return` is now contractually the **RAW active-set (Koiter) tangent** — the YF must NOT apply a tangent-operator policy. Only `MohrCoulombTensionCutoff_YF` implements the macro, so the signature change has exactly one implementor and one call site. | [#747](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/747) |
| `SRC/material/nD/ASDPlasticMaterial3D/ASDPlasticMaterial3D.h` | `// Ladruno (ADR-84 P3)`: at the `special_return` call site in `Backward_Euler`, (a) apply the material's configured `INT_OPT_tangent_operator_type` to the raw tangent the hook returns — `Elastic → Eelastic`, `Continuum`/`Algorithmic` → raw, `Secant` (**the default**) → `(D+E)/2`, `Numerical_Algorithmic_*` → raw (they differentiate `compute_local_stress()`, which never calls the hook, so they would be strictly worse); and (b) when the hook reports `SR_QUALITY_FALLBACK` (the terminal vertex projection, which discards the deviatoric state) and `strict_convergence` is on, print an `opserr` line and return -1 instead of committing it. **P0 assigned `Stiffness = stiff_sr` unconditionally and the YF had already secant-blended it, so `tangent_type` was silently overridden on every hook-resolved Gauss point** — the measured cause of the Cerro Lindo M5 confined-shear stall (87% tangent error at the corner; ADR-84 §9). Default `Secant` keeps this byte-identical. | [#747](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/747) |
| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesOutputCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-85 T0` (2D contact declarations): three edit blocks in the contact parsers. (1) `ladrunoContactSurfaceImpl` -- `-master` / `-slave-segments` accept `nodesPerSeg 2` (a 2D LINE segment; the gate was `>= 3`), and a new declaration-time block over the already-resolved node tags enforces DIMENSION CONSISTENCY (every node of one surface the same `getCrds().Size()` in {2,3} -- a MIXED set is the historical `getCrds()(2)` out-of-bounds reproducer) plus the arity<->dimension pairing (`nps 2` <=> 2D nodes, `nps 3/4` <=> 3D nodes), which is what keeps a 3D deck from declaring 2-node segments now that the `>= 3` gate is open. The dimension ORACLE is the referenced nodes' coordinates, NEVER `OPS_GetNDM()`: interpreter ndm is mutable across repeated `model` commands without wiping the domain, and on the classic Tcl path it resolves through a static `TclModelBuilder*` with no null guard (ADR-85 How/8). (2) new file-static helper `ladrunoCountLeadingNumbers(cap)` -- peeks and un-reads the leading numeric run so `contactPlane` can disambiguate its layouts; it classifies a token as an option FLAG only when `-` is followed by a LETTER, so a negative number is never mistaken for a flag, and everything else counts as a number. **Correcting the first draft of this row:** `OPS_GetString()` NEVER returns null -- `OpenSeesCommands.cpp:1202-1211` intercepts the interpreter's 0 and returns the literal `"Invalid String Input!"`. On the Python path a numeric argument therefore arrives as that literal (cursor already advanced), which is not `-`-prefixed and so counts as a number -- the right answer, but by accident of the literal's spelling, now written down rather than relied on silently. The same literal is what END-OF-INPUT returns, so a null/sentinel token is useless as a terminator and the loop is bounded by `OPS_GetNumRemainingInputArgs()` (load-bearing). Known limitation of the not-`-<letter>`-is-a-number direction: a dropped-dash typo (`visc` for `-visc`) counts as a number and surfaces as the double-read failure rather than the option loop's `unexpected token` message -- loud either way, just less specific. See [[LEDGER_quirks]]. (3) `ladrunoContactPlaneImpl` -- the slave-surface lookup moves UP (after the two ints, before the doubles) so the plane's dimension comes from the same node-coordinate oracle; on a 2D surface a 5-number `nx ny px py kn` form is accepted and a 7-number legacy form must carry `nz == pz == 0` exactly (a nonzero out-of-plane component is refused by name); the arity floor drops 9 -> 7 at ENTRY, but a non-2D plane is re-checked against the SHIPPED floor of 9 once the dimension is resolved and gets the shipped usage line back verbatim -- so a 3D 7- or 8-argument typo still reports usage, not a downstream `could not read nx ny nz px py pz kn`. The same branch appends one clarifying line when the slave-surface peek found NO surface, which is the genuinely confusing case: the 7-arg 2D form written before its `contactSurface` exists (dimension unknown => 3D assumed), where blaming the argument count would be a lie. An unresolvable surface leaves the dimension at 3, so every shipped refusal (`define the slave contactSurface first`, `addRigidPlane`'s `slave surface N not defined`) still fires with its shipped text in its shipped order. **3D decks are byte-identical**: the new checks are either dimension-neutral (all-3D surfaces pass silently) or reached only under `planeDim == 2` / `nodesPerSeg == 2`. Refusal messages carry the literals `ADR-85` + `dimension` / `nodesPerSeg` per the ADR-85 How/8 guard table. | [#749](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/749) -- ADR-85 T0 |

| `SRC/interpreter/OpenSeesOutputCommands.cpp` | `// Ladruno ADR-85 F1` (`-outward winding`): FOUR edit blocks in `ladrunoContactImpl`, all additive. (1) the `outwardWinding` local + its option-table comment; (2) inside the EXISTING `owDim == 2` arm of the `-outward` branch, a one-token keyword peek before the shipped 2-double read -- read one token, compare to `winding`, un-read it if it does not match. The peek hazards are this file's own (`ladrunoCountLeadingNumbers`'s block comment): `OPS_GetString()` NEVER returns null (it hands back the literal `"Invalid String Input!"` when the interpreter has no string, e.g. a PyFloat on the Python path) and it CONSUMES the token on both paths, so the only safe classification is read-compare-unread. A numeric `ox` -- `"0.0"` on Tcl, the sentinel literal on Python -- never equals `winding`, so the shipped 2-double read is reached verbatim; (3) in the `owDim == 3` arm, the SAME peek used only to intercept `winding` BY NAME (otherwise it falls into the 3-double read and dies with `need ox oy oz`, naming a layout the user never wrote -- the T1b complaint from the other side). The token is un-read either way, so the shipped 3D read and its message are textually untouched; (4) two post-loop cross-option refusals -- `winding` with an explicit `-outward` (two declarations of the same fact; the engine never picks one), and `winding` with `-mortar` (the mortar lane runs no chain-integrity scan, so winding's one-connected-chain invariant is not established there; flush 2D MORTAR interfaces still need `-outward ox oy`). Checked post-loop because `-mortar` is itself a flag and may be written either side of `-outward`. Plus the `addContact` call-site's 18th argument. **3D decks are byte-identical**: the new peek is reached only under `-outward`, consumes nothing unless the token IS `winding`, and `winding` on a 3D pair is refused before any state changes. Refusal messages carry `ADR-85` + `winding` / `mortar` per the F1 test contract. | [#764](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/764) -- ADR-85 F1 |

| `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/ManzariDafalias.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno (ADR-86 PR-2, D7)` **commit 1 of 7 — the `mTolR` flag seam.** `ManzariDafalias::ModifiedEuler()` (IntScheme 1, the recommended error-controlled explicit integrator) opened with `double T = 0.0, dT = 1.0, dT_min = 1e-6 , TolE = 1e-4;` — a **hardcoded** substep error tolerance that ignores the user's `TolR` completely. The sibling `RungeKutta45()` in the same file uses `TolE = mTolR` (`:1771`) and so does `SAniSandMS` (`:1484`), so a deck that passes a tight `TolR` on scheme 1 is silently running error control at `1e-4` while its input says otherwise. **This commit only opens the seam, it does not wire anything to it** (per ADR §4.1: a protected member on the base, defaulting to the vanilla value, read at the defect site, set only from a derived constructor — never a `virtual` promotion, which would convert `ManzariDafaliasRO`'s `initialize()`/`GetElasticModuli` *shadows* at `ManzariDafaliasRO.h:93-97` into overrides and change every Ramberg-Osgood deck). Two edits: (1) `.h` — new protected `bool mHonorTolRInME;` beside `mStressCorrectionInUse`, named **deliberately distinct** from `LadrunoSANISAND`'s `int mHonorTolR` (the deck-level request; this is the base-side seam it acts on) so the two stay greppable apart; (2) `.cpp` — the `TolE` initialiser becomes `mHonorTolRInME ? mTolR : 1e-4`, plus `mHonorTolRInME = false;` in **all four** `ManzariDafalias` constructors (`:132` full/ND-tag, `:209` full/classTag, `:284` classTag-only, `:338` null) — every one, because missing one would leave an uninitialised `bool` reading indeterminate memory. **Vanilla is bit-identical:** the flag is false in all four constructors and is written nowhere in the class, so the conditional operator selects the `1e-4` operand verbatim — no arithmetic, no rounding, and no other value in `ModifiedEuler` reads the flag. Verified by rebuild + the 12-passed/1-skipped `test_ladruno_sanisand.py` + `test_manzari_safety_pack.py` battery, unchanged. See [[LEDGER_quirks]]. | ADR-86 PR-2 |
| `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/ManzariDafalias.cpp` | `// Ladruno (ADR-86 PR-2)` **commit 2 of 7 — make the low-`p` stress clamp observable (ADR §6 "PR-2 clamp warning \| Diagnostics only").** Two integrator entry guards silently REBUILD the stress when the mean pressure falls under the floor — `ModifiedEuler()` (`p < m_Pmin + m_Presidual` → `NextStress = GetDevPart(NextStress) + m_Pmin*mI1`) and `RungeKutta45()` (`p < m_Pmin`, same rebuild; note it computes `p` *without* `m_Presidual`, unlike ModifiedEuler). Both carried a notice gated on `debugFlag`, which is a `static const bool` **defined `false`** at `:56` — a recompile-only switch, so **neither notice has ever printed for any user**, and the clamp has been changing answers with no trace at all. Added an ungated `opserr` at both sites naming the material tag, the offending `p`, and the threshold it fell under, stating plainly that the point's result is set by the clamp and not by the model. **Throttle, and why it is not "once per instance":** the sites sit inside a `while (T < 1.0)` substep loop inside a Gauss-point update inside a Newton iteration inside a load step, and every Gauss point is its own material instance (`getCopy(const char*)` runs a full constructor per integration point — ADR §4.4's refinement box measured ~400k instances / 83 MB of stderr for a per-instance echo on a 50k-element model during PR-1). The throttle is therefore a **process-wide budget**: a function-local `static int`, first 10 events print, the 10th also prints a suppression notice, then silence. **Worst case is 11 lines per site, 22 total, for a mesh of any size.** Diagnostics only — not one numeric operation changed; battery unchanged at 12 passed / 1 skipped. **Three further `p`-floor sites were found and deliberately NOT instrumented** (reported, not hidden): `explicit_integrator()` `:1042` and `BackwardEuler_CPPM()` `:2102` are *illegal-state bail-outs* (they discard the deviator entirely — `NextStress = m_Pmin*mI1`, `NextAlpha.Zero()`), a different failure with a different message, and `Stress_Correction()` `:2444` clamps only its local `p` and has no diagnostic to un-gate. Instrumenting them is a separate decision. See [[LEDGER_quirks]]. | ADR-86 PR-2 |
| `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/ManzariDafalias.cpp` | `// Ladruno (ADR-86 PR-2, D5b)` **commit 3 of 7 — non-dimensionalise the `D_factor` dilatancy sigmoid, all four sites together.** `D_factor = 1.0/(1.0 + exp(7.6349 - 7.2713*p))` multiplies a **raw stress** by a bare literal, so `7.2713` silently carries units of 1/stress while the trigger `p < 0.05*m_P_atm` beside it scales correctly: at 1 kPa true confinement the factor is **0.410 in kPa, 1.000 in Pa (never fires), 0.0005 in MPa (total suppression)** — three different materials from one input file, and OpenSees has no unit system to catch it. Rewritten as `b/m_P_atm` with `b = 7.2713 * 101.0`, `a = 7.6349` unchanged. **Found by grep, not by the ADR's line numbers, and the count matched: four sites** — `GetStateDependent` (`:4838`, the value only) and three byte-identical replicas inside the analytical Jacobian lane (`NewtonSol` `:3082`, `NewtonSol_negP` `:3897`, `GetJacobian` `:4362`), each of which carries **its own derivative** `- one3*Macauley(D) * be * temp1/pow(1+temp1,2) * mI1`. The derivative is picked up for free because `be` **is** `d(exponent)/dp`: redefining `double be = 7.2713 * 101.0 / m_P_atm;` and routing `temp1` and `D_factor` through it rescales value and slope in one edit, so the consistent Jacobian keeps linearising the residual it is meant to linearise. The three Jacobian blocks were patched with a single replace-all over the identical 3-line block, so they cannot drift apart. **The constant is spelled `7.2713 * 101.0`, NOT the ADR's rounded `734.401`** — `734.401/101 = 7.27129703`, a 4.1e-7 relative shift, i.e. the rounded literal would NOT have been the no-op the ADR promises. **No-op proven, not asserted:** (a) `(7.2713*101.0)/101.0 == 7.2713` bit-for-bit in IEEE double, so the whole expression is literally the same double; (b) 500 000 random `p` over the full trigger domain `[0, 0.05*P_atm]` plus the endpoints — zero mismatches in value AND in the derivative term; (c) a 1200-step low-`p` drained triaxial at `p0 = 0.01*P_atm` (the whole run inside the sigmoid's active branch) returns **bit-identical** committed stress, hex for hex, on both the vanilla and the `p_r = 0` legs. Behaviour changes only for a deck that declared its atmosphere in some other unit, and for that deck the present behaviour is the defect. **Disclosure owed (ADR §7.2.1):** a deck declaring `P_atm = 101.3` rather than `101.0` shifts ~1.3 % in `D_factor` at 1 kPa, because its declared atmosphere differs from the one the bare constant implicitly assumed. **`101.0` and not `101.325` on purpose** — the target is exact reproduction at the value the constant was calibrated against, which the ADR and the Gorini parameter set both give as `101.0`. Siblings **deliberately untouched** (scope decision not taken): `SAniSandMS.cpp:2627` carries the identical bare sigmoid at one site with no Jacobian replicas — and its trigger is `p < 0.001*m_P_atm`, verified, not `0.05`; `NTUASand02.cpp` is **not present in this tree** at all despite ADR §7.2 citing it. | `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/ManzariDafalias.cpp` | `// Ladruno (ADR-86 PR-2)` **commit 4 of 7 — the substep void-ratio interpolant started from the WRONG END, at all three substepping integrators.** `T` sweeps 0->1 across the increment, so the void ratio at substep pseudo-time `T` must be evaluated at `CurStrain + T*(NextStrain - CurStrain)`; `ModifiedEuler` (`:1411`), `RungeKutta4` (`:1667`) and `RungeKutta45` (`:1896`) all evaluated at **`NextStrain`** + T*(...) — a full increment ahead at `T=0`. The §6 row's original "~0.012 % on `M^b`" was never reproduced and is corrected in place. **`RungeKutta4` does not move; `RungeKutta45` DOES.** The original claim that both were inert was REFUTED by an adversarial pre-merge review and independently re-verified: RK4's loop reads a separate `CurVoidRatio` (7 in-loop reads, 0 of `NextVoidRatio`), so there the bad interpolant only corrupted the RETURNED void ratio -- but **RK45's loop reads `NextVoidRatio` at all 11 in-loop `GetStateDependent`/`GetElasticModuli` sites and `CurVoidRatio` at none**, so RK45 is a genuine behaviour change. Measured by A/B rebuild on a NON-isochoric drained triaxial: scheme 1 and scheme 3 bit-identical, **scheme 45 reldiff 6.33e-3** (sigma_zz -1204.82 vs -1209.38 kPa). PR-2's own measurement reported zero because the confine-first test deck's deviator leg is **exactly isochoric** (trace(d_eps) = 0) and the two interpolant anchors differ by exactly trace(d_eps) -- true on that deck, falsely generalised. All three fixed in one commit because they are one defect; patching one replica and not its siblings is the trap §7.2 documents for the sigmoid. **Approved by the user before the edit**, per WORKFLOW_GOTCHAS §6's rule for genuine upstream bugfixes. `SAniSandMS.cpp:1550` carries the identical error and is deliberately left to that material's own PR.
