| `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/ManzariDafalias.cpp` | `// Ladruno (ADR-86 PR-2)` **commit 5 of 7 — the `ModifiedEuler()` low-`p` clamp rebuilt the stress and the scalar `p` to DIFFERENT values.** The guard is `p < m_Pmin + m_Presidual` and the recovery was `NextStress = GetDevPart(NextStress) + m_Pmin*mI1; p = m_Pmin;`. Throughout `ModifiedEuler` `p` means `one3*GetTrace(sigma) + m_Presidual` (`:1359`, `:1420`, `:1479`, `:1564`), and the rebuilt tensor has `one3*GetTrace(NextStress) == m_Pmin` exactly — so the value consistent with the tensor is `m_Pmin + m_Presidual`, **1.0201 vs the 0.0101 that was written, a factor of 101** at the vanilla defaults. The site was also the family outlier: `Stress_Correction()` (`:2521`) writes `m_Pmin + m_Presidual` at its analogous clamp and `RungeKutta45()` (`:1888`) recomputes `p` from the rebuilt stress. Replaced with the recompute form `p = one3*GetTrace(NextStress) + m_Presidual;` — mirroring RK45 rather than restating a literal, so the scalar cannot drift if the rebuild above it is ever changed. **THE STORE WAS DEAD, and the fix is therefore numerically INERT — this is the finding, not an excuse.** `p` is a local; the `while (T < 1.0)` loop directly below is always entered (`T == 0.0` at that point) and the first statement in it that touches `p` is the unconditional recompute at `:1420`; every line between the clamp and that recompute was read and none of them reads `p`. Proven twice: (a) statically, by that inspection; (b) empirically, on a 7-leg low-`p` drained-triaxial fingerprint (full 26-entry state vector, all 6 stress components, a 120-point stress trail, and the post-confinement stress, at full `repr` precision) whose JSON is **byte-identical before and after — same MD5 `815c88e6…`** — and which includes THREE legs with `-Pmin` raised (1.5 / 2.2 / 3.0 kPa) specifically so the clamp branch is entered: the PR-2 clamp diagnostic fires its full 10-event budget on both runs, and one of those legs completes all 1200 steps and lands on identical numbers. The clamp does **not** fire at all on the ADR §5 deck at vanilla constants. Fixed anyway: a dead wrong value is one inserted read away from being a live wrong value, and this block — which now carries PR-2's clamp diagnostic — is precisely where the next reader gets added. **Deliberately NOT changed, and reported instead of hidden:** `Stress_Correction()`'s clamp differs from `ModifiedEuler()`'s in two further ways — it collapses the deviator (`NextStress = p * mI1`, `NextAlpha.Zero()`) where ModifiedEuler preserves it, and the tensor it writes has mean stress `m_Pmin + m_Presidual` where ModifiedEuler's has `m_Pmin`, so it lands strictly inside the floor where ModifiedEuler lands exactly on it. Both are self-consistent recovery strategies for different situations (a last-resort return-map corrector vs a pre-loop sanity floor); making them identical would be a redesign of a LIVE return value, which ADR-86 D9 classes as opinion, not error. | `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/ManzariDafalias.{h,cpp}` | `// Ladruno (ADR-86 PR-2, D9)` **commit 7 of 7 — the `m_e_init` elastic-`G` FLAG SEAM (ADR §7.3). Opened, deliberately NOT wired, and NOT shipped as a plain bugfix.** Dafalias & Manzari (2004) p.623 give `G = G0·p_at·(2.97-e)²/(1+e)·(p/p_at)^½` with `e` the **CURRENT** void ratio. All three `ManzariDafalias::GetElasticModuli` overloads used `m_e_init`, the **INITIAL** one, at **six lines / twelve token occurrences** (two lines per overload, `mElastFlag == 0` branch and `else` branch) — while the function is handed the current void ratio as parameter `en` and did not use it at all, **the correct line sits commented out** inside the disabled block three lines above the first site, `b0` in the same file uses the current `e`, and `SAniSandMS` uses `en`. **Why this is not a bugfix commit:** it moves a CALIBRATED quantity — the reporting project's `G0 = 264.32` was fitted against the frozen `m_e_init` form, so correcting `G` without revisiting `G0` would preserve one error by means of another. ADR-86 D9 sends exactly this case to the flag seam. **Shape, mirroring commit 1's `mTolR` seam:** (1) `.h` — new protected `bool mUseCurrentVoidRatioInG;` beside `mHonorTolRInME`; (2) `.cpp` — `mUseCurrentVoidRatioInG = false;` in **all four** `ManzariDafalias` constructors (`:132` full/ND-tag, `:218` full/classTag, `:302` classTag-only, `:363` null; missing one would leave an uninitialised `bool`), and each of the three overloads opens with `const double& eG = mUseCurrentVoidRatioInG ? en : m_e_init;` with the two `G` lines reading `eG`. **A reference, not a copy** — both conditional operands are `double` lvalues, so `eG` names `m_e_init` itself: no conversion, no temporary, no rounding step. **Nothing is wired**: `grep -rn mUseCurrentVoidRatioInG SRC/` returns hits in `ManzariDafalias.cpp` (8) and `ManzariDafalias.h` (2) and **nowhere else** — not `LadrunoSANISAND`, not the parser. **BIT-IDENTITY PROVEN, NOT ASSERTED, on two independent decks:** (a) the 7-leg low-`p` drained-triaxial fingerprint from commit 5 (full 26-entry state vector, 6 stress components, 120-point trail, post-confinement stress, full precision) is unchanged at MD5 `815c88e6…`; (b) a **Ramberg-Osgood** Tcl deck (`nDMaterial ManzariDafaliasRO`, unit `stdBrick`, elastic confinement + 300 plastic steps) — 52 numeric output lines at `%.17g`, unchanged at MD5 `aac92070…`; the only bytes that moved in that file were the splash banner's git SHA and the shell's own command echo. **THE RO HAZARD, and how it was discharged:** `ManzariDafaliasRO` (`ManzariDafaliasRO.h:93-97`) *shadows* `initialize()` and **two of these three** overloads — the 5-arg one and `(sigma, en, K, G)` — with identical signatures. **No `virtual` was added**; a data member cannot turn a shadow into an override. Confirmed four ways: RO's own bodies (`ManzariDafaliasRO.cpp:269`, `:305`) compute `Gmax = m_B*m_P_atm/(0.3+0.7*en*en)*sqrt(p/m_P_atm)` and never mention `m_e_init` (its only two mentions in that file are `getCopy` clone arguments) or the new flag; both RO constructors delegate to a `ManzariDafalias` constructor, so the flag is initialised on every RO path; the header edit forced a **recompile of `ManzariDafaliasRO`, `ManzariDafalias3DRO` and `ManzariDafaliasPlaneStrainRO`** (verified in the build log — no stale-object illusion); and the RO deck above is a live A/B. That deck is an honest test of an EDITED body too, not just of RO's: the **third** overload `(sigma, en, K, G, const double& D)` is the one RO does *not* shadow, and `ManzariDafalias::commitState:464` calls it on every commit — so one run exercises RO's own elasticity and a seamed base body together. Battery: 13 passed / 1 skipped (up from 12/1 — a sibling agent added a test; nothing failed and nothing regressed); `check_classtags` OK (3 warns), `check_manifest` OK (4 warns). See [[LEDGER_quirks]]. | ADR-86 PR-2 |
| `SRC/material/nD/UWmaterials/ManzariDafalias.cpp` | `// Ladruno` **`OPS_ManzariDafaliasMaterial` wrote PAST a stack array on a deck with more than five trailing optionals.** `numArgs` is `19 + k` for `k` optionals, so `numData = numArgs - 19` is `k` — **uncapped** — and it was passed straight to `OPS_GetDouble`. `OPS_GetDoubleInput` (`SRC/api/elementAPI_TCL.cpp:316-329`) loops `for (i = 0; i < *numData; i++) data[i] = ...` with **no bound on the destination**, stopping only when the input args run out or a token fails to parse. The destination is `double oData[5]` (`:90`). So `nDMaterial ManzariDafalias 1 <18 doubles> 1 0 1 1e-7 1e-7 0 0 0` (k = 8) wrote `oData[0..7]` into a `double[5]` — **24 bytes over, on the stack**. Added a `numData > 5` guard that REFUSES the deck. **Refuses rather than truncates on purpose:** a silent truncation would run the deck with arguments the user did not get, which is exactly the ADR-86 §7.1 defect class (`OPS_SAniSandMSMaterial` silently dropping `TolF`/`TolR`) that this repair exists not to repeat. **Found by adversarial review during ADR-86 PR-3** (as an out-of-scope aside from the registration/parser lens), not by the ADR's own audit. **Gate:** `tests/test_manzari_safety_pack.py::test_optional_arg_count_is_bounded`, a genuine fail-before/pass-after test — pre-fix the eight trailing values are all valid doubles, so `OPS_GetDouble` reads all eight, returns success, and the material is CONSTRUCTED with no exception and no warning; the test asserts the refusal and therefore fails pre-fix. **Proven by mutation:** replacing the guard with `if (false)` fails that test and only that test. Nothing in the gate depends on observing the out-of-bounds write, which is undefined behaviour. **FAMILY SWEEP, surveyed and deliberately NOT fixed here** (reported, not hidden): the identical uncapped pattern is in `ManzariDafaliasRO.cpp:82` (`numArgs - 22` into `double oData[6]`), `PM4Sand.cpp:128` (`numArgs - 5` into `oData[24]`) and `PM4Silt.cpp:129` (`numArgs - 6` into `oData[24]`). `SAniSandMS.cpp:134` is **safe** from this — it bounds its reads with `std::min(numData, 3)` / `std::min(numData, 2)` — though it still carries the separate §7.1 silent-drop defect. Those three are one coordinated change with their own regression tests and are a scope decision, not an oversight. `OPS_LadrunoSANISAND` is immune to this whole family by construction: it never computes a count. Approved by the user before the edit, per `WORKFLOW_GOTCHAS` §6. See [[LEDGER_quirks]]. | ADR-86 follow-up |

| `CMakeLists.txt`, `SRC/system_of_eqn/linearSOE/CMakeLists.txt` | `// Ladruno (TIMs, 2026-09-03)` **`system Pardiso` was Windows-only by accident of the build, not of the solver.** ADR-75 P1b gates `PARDISO_FLAG` on `MKL_LPATH`, which is assigned only in the Windows branch from `.lib` names, so no MKL on Linux could enable it; the serial Linux build fell back to UmfPack, whose 32-bit index path fails (`numeric analysis returns -1`, 28 GB free) between 49 626 and 93 246 DOF on the TIMs `LadrunoUP` footing, whose 64-bit path never runs the symbolic analysis in this build, and where SuperLU took >18x UmfPack's whole gravity stage on the smaller mesh without finishing a solve. New opt-in `LADRUNO_MKL_PARDISO_LINUX` (default OFF, byte-identical otherwise) mirrors `LADRUNO_MKL_FEAST_LINUX`: explicit LP64/**sequential**/core 3-layer through `MKL_RT_HINT` plus `mkl_pardiso.h`, attached to `OPS_SysOfEqn` PUBLIC; the `pardiso/` subdirectory gate accepts it beside `MKL_FOUND`. Sequential layer on purpose — the Linux targets already link sequential MKL and one process carries one threading layer. Gate: on esmeralda, `mid` (49 626 DOF) `Pardiso` vs `UmfPack` ten push steps to 1e-6, then the 93 246-DOF wide box's elastic gravity stage solving. | pardiso-linux |
| `CMakeLists.txt` (root) | `# Ladruno (TIMs, 2026-09-03, second opt-in)`: **`option(LADRUNO_MKL_PARDISO_LINUX_THREADED OFF)`** — swaps the Linux PARDISO opt-in's MKL layer from `mkl_sequential` to `mkl_gnu_thread` + `libgomp`; refuses to configure if `LAPACK_LIBRARIES`/`BLAS_LIBRARIES` still name `mkl_sequential`, and (#782 verification) refuses `LADRUNO_MKL_FEAST_LINUX=ON` beside it, because the FEAST block resolves `mkl_sequential` into the same cached `LADRUNO_MKL_SEQ` and the threaded lookup would silently no-op. Default OFF ⇒ every existing build byte-identical. Verified on esmeralda (spack oneAPI MKL 2024.2): links `libmkl_gnu_thread.so.2` + `libgomp.so.1`, no sequential layer; 16³/24³ stdBrick block matches UmfPack to all printed digits; 46 875 DOF solve 3.39 s → 1.44 s at 1 → 8 threads. | [#782](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/782) |
| `SRC/analysis/fe_ele/transformation/TransformationFE.cpp`, `SRC/analysis/fe_ele/transformation/TransformationFE.h` | `// Ladruno (ADR-80 P3)`: **new `getSPTangentForce(Integrator*)`** — the Kratos-style `b -= K*du_D` term, the piece a Transformation-eliminated DOF can never contribute through `getResidual()`. Builds `du` in the element's ORIGINAL dof space (zero except sp'd dofs, where it holds *prescribed total − committed nodal value*), forms `f = K du` with the element tangent at the **committed** state, and returns `T^t f` in the assembled dof space so the caller can post it with the element's ordinary `getID()` — whose `-1` entries drop the eliminated rows for free. Returns `0` (assembles nothing) when the element has no sp'd node **or** when every sp on it is homogeneous (`du == 0`), so plain `fix` boundaries cost one virtual call and no tangent formation. **Finding worth keeping: the pre-existing `getTangForce(const Vector &, double)` stub CANNOT be the hook ADR-80 D6 assumed it was** — it indexes a GLOBAL vector by `myID`, and an eliminated dof has `myID == -1`, so the prescribed increment is not representable in its argument at all. The stub is left untouched. **Purely additive: no stock caller, zero behaviour change unless `LadrunoLoadControl -tangentPredictor` is used.** | [#786](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/786) |

Deliberately mirrors `enforceSPs`'s value convention line for line so the two can never disagree about what "the prescribed increment" means. Warns when a node carries **both** an sp and an mp, because `enforceSPs(0)` then overwrites the sp from the retained node and the computed increment is not what the node will receive. Additive. | [#786](https://github.com/nmorabowen/OpenSees/pull/786) |
